SpikesPeak — Running Status Log#

PROJECT: SpikesPeak - Real-Time Neural Data Acquisition System

RUNNING STATUS LOG -- the canonical, append-as-we-go record of where the project stands and where it is going. Began as the Neuropixels 1.0 / IMRO status record (this branch's original purpose) and now also tracks cross-cutting architecture work (module communication) and the planned overhauls. Renamed from SpikesPeak_IMRO_Status.txt on 2026-06-25 as scope grew past IMRO.

STATUS DATE: 2026-08-21 -- Neuropixels OneBox is a working acquisition source. A real NP2.0 multishank probe on a real OneBox connects, streams, records, pauses/plays, disconnects and RECONNECTS cleanly, with its 12-channel ADC streaming alongside as its own live column in real volts. Tip referencing is now a GUI toggle for NP1.0 and NP2.0. NP1.0-on-OneBox is the next hardware test.

PRIOR MILESTONE (2026-07-21) -- BOTH probes' closed loops fully quantified: the complete quorum x delivery matrix at 1000 detections per cell on NP1.0 AND NP2.0. Sub-millisecond with ASAP delivery at every quorum level on both -- NP1.0 0.94-0.98 ms, NP2.0 0.55-0.61 ms. ASAP now works on NP2.0 (Npx2MSProbe::setLowLatency). The closed loop is validated end to end on both probes.

SEE ALSO: developer_docs/README.md (the doc map + the START HERE path for a new agent); developer_docs/archive/README.md (retired docs + how to catch up); user_docs/ (GUI usage); images/architecture_overview.md (architecture.txt / plantuml_export.txt)

An NP1.0 on the OneBox stopped delivering data and the software reported a fully healthy rig for 3.5 min, then could not be shut down. Three defects: liveness counted timer ticks not data (so SourceManager's stall watchdog could never fire); SUCCESS with 0 superframes matched neither the data nor the error branch (silent by construction); and np_arm() ran bare on the main thread, killing the Qt event loop and every shutdown path. All three fixed and verified on the failing hardware — the stall now reaches the GUI in 3 s and the app exits cleanly in 9 s where it used to hang forever. output.log now rotates.

Non-fatal by default, -DSPIKESPEAK_TESTS_FATAL=ON for CI. Verified from a clean tree. Blocked on broader coverage: Utils.cpp is the composition root — extracting a SpikesPeakCore static lib is the next high-leverage step.

Now delay-loaded and detected at runtime (NiDaqRuntime.*), so one binary runs on both rigs. Chrome path in _harness.py made discovered; the original box's C:\Users\BIOEl\... path removed from 4 scripts; HDF5 is 2.2.0 not 1.14.6 and needs CMAKE_PREFIX_PATH; VS2022 builds the msvc2019_64 Qt kit fine. Open: the real ADC opens (device round-trip) but reports only to the log, and its values cannot be validated without a known BNC signal.

The one that mattered: a re-enabled band served STALE samples — BandFilterProcessor indexed its output ring by the count of samples it had READ, not by what it had COMMITTED. Also: a refused source select reported itself as a switch, and a missing audio device could stop acquisition.

Where the detail lives. This log records what happened and when. The how it works lives in the living topic docs under developer_docs/ (see its README index). Retired snapshots are in developer_docs/archive/ with a table saying what superseded each one.

1. ARCHITECTURAL OVERVIEW#

SpikesPeak is a modular C++20/Qt6 backend with a JavaScript/SciChart frontend that runs in an EXTERNAL browser: the backend serves the UI over HTTP :8080 and opens the system browser; all backchannel traffic rides ONE WebSocket on :1234, triple-multiplexed into (a) a binary waveform stream, (b) JSON app commands, and (c) Qt QWebChannel RPC. Per-source controls (the IMRO editor) are Q_INVOKABLE methods on the source object, exposed over QWebChannel. Full detail: developer_docs/SpikesPeak_Architecture_Review.md.

2. FEATURE GOAL#

Interactive Neuropixels channel-map (IMRO) configuration from the browser UI: visualize the probe, select which electrodes/channels to record, load/save .imro files, and push the map to hardware. Focus so far: Neuropixels 1.0 (960 electrodes -> 384 read-out channels). NP2.0 multishank is next.

3. NEUROPIXELS 1.0 ELECTRODE MODEL (implemented)#

  • 960 physical electrodes feed 384 read-out channels by modulo-384 wiring: channel C = electrode % 384 ; bank B = floor(electrode / 384) ; electrode = C + 384*B.
  • Each read-out channel carries exactly ONE electrode, so selecting an electrode for channel C deselects its bank-siblings automatically (the model is 384 channel objects, one bank each).
  • Bank 2 is a HALF-bank: electrodes 768-959 only => valid for channels 0-191 only (191 + 384*2 = 959 exists; 192 + 768 = 960 does not).
  • RESERVED channel 191: its three bank electrodes (0-indexed 191/575/959 = the manual's 1-indexed 192/576/960, spaced 3.84 mm) are the internal-reference sites, not recording electrodes. The UI draws them amber and blocks selecting them; applyImroToHardware skips np_selectElectrode for 191.
  • Reference IDs: {0=ext, 1=tip, 2..4=on-shank-ref at banks 0/1/2}; applyImroToHardware maps these to EXT_REF / TIP_REF / INT_REF(bank). The .imro NP1 entry is: (channelID bank refID apGain lfGain apFilter)

4. C++ BACKEND (implemented, NpInterface.cpp/.hpp)#

  • Data model ImroTable / ImroChannel (inline-initialized; no indeterminate fields).
  • SIX Q_INVOKABLE methods on NeuropixelsSource, all reached over QWebChannel: getImroAsJson() - serialize current map -> JSON for the UI getImroAsImroText() - serialize current map -> .imro TEXT (browser downloads it; the UI Save) updateImroFromJson() - validate + atomically replace the map (channel/bank range + NP1 electrode-existence checks; rejects bad payloads without corruption) applyMapToHardware() - push current map to the API + np_writeProbeConfiguration loadImroFromText(text) - parse .imro file CONTENTS (read client-side by the browser) into the map saveImroToFile(path) - SERVER-SIDE write to an explicit path (programmatic; not used by the UI)
  • IMRO parsing refactored: parseImroFile() (reads a file) -> parseImroContent() (parses a string). The parser parses into LOCALS, validates, then commits ATOMICALLY -- this fixed a latent bug that corrupted mCurrentImro on a failed/incompatible load (it overwrote the header before the probe-type check). NP1 entries are range-validated (channel in [0,384), electrode < 960).
  • Hardware abstraction: np_selectElectrode / np_setGain / np_setReference / np_setAPCornerFrequency, with every return code checked; channel 191 electrode-selection skipped (reserved; constant kNp1ReservedChannel).
  • Lifetime/safety hardening from the 2026-06-22 review applied (worker teardown + wait, member init, leak-free init-failure paths, per-instance scratch buffer, etc.).

5. JS FRONTEND (implemented, ImroManager.js)#

  • SciChart probe map: dark-mode WebGL scatter of the NP1.0 geometry; green = selected recording electrode, gray = available, amber = internal-reference site (191/575/959).
  • Selection (all honor the modulo-384 rules; reserved channel 191 is never modified):
    • CLICK an electrode -> route its channel to that electrode's bank (siblings auto-deselect).
    • DRAG a vertical box -> select a contiguous 384-electrode window (always a valid full map).
    • Bank 0/1/2 presets (Bank 2 = half-bank: channels 0-191 -> bank 2, 192-383 -> bank 0). Edits push to C++ via updateImroFromJson() and re-render WITHOUT re-zooming (no view jump).
  • Apply Map button -> applyMapToHardware() (Q_INVOKABLE).
  • Save .imro button -> getImroAsImroText() -> the browser downloads the .imro CLIENT-SIDE (Blob). No server dialog (symmetric with Load). main.cpp sets quitOnLastWindowClosed(false) defensively.
  • Load .imro button -> browser reads the file CLIENT-SIDE, hands the text to loadImroFromText(); on success the UI re-fetches + re-renders (auto-zoom to the loaded block) so the loaded channels light up. Load does NOT auto-apply -- the user clicks Apply to push to HW.
  • Handshake: NetworkClient waits for the C++ deviceReady event before binding QWebChannel, then binds window.activeNpxModule by registry-name substring ("npx").

6. KEY ARCHITECTURAL DECISIONS#

  • Per-module commands ride QWebChannel Q_INVOKABLE (Option A): each source registers its own object and exposes its own invokables, so new modules (e.g. a camera) need no central command-router edit. The legacy handleWebCommand JSON path was removed (it was dead + unreachable).
  • FILE I/O STANDARD (external-browser architecture) -- now documented in developer_docs:
    • READ a file -> do it CLIENT-SIDE in the browser ( + FileReader) and pass the TEXT to a synchronous C++ Q_INVOKABLE parser. No server dialog, no event-loop re-entrancy. Reference implementation: Load .imro -> loadImroFromText().
    • WRITE a file -> CLIENT-SIDE too: a Q_INVOKABLE returns the file TEXT and the browser downloads it (Blob + ). No server dialog. Reference: Save .imro -> getImroAsImroText(). (A server-side modal dialog in a Q_INVOKABLE crashed -- nested loop re-entering QWebChannel -- and once deferred it EXITED the app: the dialog was the windowless server's only window, tripping quitOnLastWindowClosed. main.cpp now sets quitOnLastWindowClosed(false) defensively.)

7. BUILD STATUS#

  • Compiles clean (CMake/Ninja + MSVC2019 + Qt6.7.3, Release): exit 0, no warnings; webinterface is redeployed next to the exe by the POST_BUILD step. node --check passes on all webinterface/*.js.

8. LIVE DATA VIEW: DEPTH / SHANK / COLUMN + APP LIFECYCLE (this session, 2026-06-24)#

Full detail + the data-plane contract: developer_docs/LiveDataView.md.

  • The streaming waveform view now orders traces by DEPTH using the APPLIED map. Each read-out channel gets a global SLOT = its rank sorted by (shank ASC, column ASC [L before R], row ASC [deep first]), where for a within-shank electrode e: column = e&1 (even=Left, odd=Right), row = e>>1 (row 0 = tip). yOffset = slot*spacing on a NORMAL y-axis, so the TIP (deepest) sits at the BOTTOM, shallower upward.
  • Traces are labeled by ELECTRODE + side, e.g. "100L" (hover shows shank / column / read-out channel). This fixed the earlier "scrambled, non-monotonic" labels (channel order != depth order for mixed maps).
  • PER-COLUMN FILTERS: each display column has TWO dropdowns -- Shank (one entry per shank; DISABLED for NP1's single shank) and electrode column (L+R / Left / Right). The column zooms its y-axis to that contiguous slot BAND; the slider scrolls within the band. Set col1=Left, col2=Right to see both physical columns side-by-side. Non-probe sources hide the dropdowns and fall back to channel order.
  • KEY CONSTRAINT: NetworkClient bakes yOffset into the per-channel data series. Because "L+R" needs a DIFFERENT order than L/R-only (interleaved vs packed), each channel now carries TWO series -- a PACKED one (dataSeries; L/R views, depth-sorted) and an electrode-INTERLEAVED one (dataSeriesInterleaved; the L+R view, ordered 0L,1R,2L,3R...). NetworkClient appends only the series the visible columns need (default "B" => interleaved only; AppController.updateAppendModes), so it stays ~1x append in the common cases. Remaining deliberate limit: no "all shanks" option (each column = one shank); a third ordering would need per-column data (see developer_docs/LiveDataView.md S5).
  • UPDATE 2026-06-25: "L+R" now sorts by electrode number first (interleaved), per user request; L/R-only views are unchanged (packed by depth). Node self-test: interleaved first6 = 0L,1R,2L,3R,4L,5R; packed L/R bands unchanged. Deployed; awaiting hardware verification.
  • UPDATE 2026-06-25 (REF): the reserved internal-reference channel (NP1 ch 191) is now PINNED to the top of its column and labeled "REF" instead of floating to the deepest slot on non-bank-0 maps (it stays on bank 0 because the editor never re-banks it, so it was sorting to the bottom-right as the smallest active electrode). recomputeDepthOrdering uses a sentinel sort row (REF_ROW=1e6); imroLiveView.isRef drives the label. Node self-test: ch191 -> R-band top / interleaved top, never slot 0. JS-only; deployed.
  • APP LIFECYCLE: WebSocketServer now runs a single-shot 2 s grace timer -- when the LAST browser (WebSocket client) disconnects it schedules QCoreApplication::quit(); a reconnect (page refresh) within the window cancels it. So closing the browser closes the backend, but a refresh does not, and the console no longer has to be closed by hand. Coexists with quitOnLastWindowClosed(false).
  • FILES TOUCHED: webinterface/ImroManager.js (recomputeDepthOrdering + window.imroLiveView ordering/ bands), webinterface/main.js (relayoutByDepth -> slot yOffsets + refreshFilterOptions hooks), webinterface/ChartColumn.js (dropdowns, band/slot draw, orientation, electrode+side labels), src/core/WebSocketServer.hpp/.cpp (grace-timer auto-quit). C++ rebuilt + linked clean.

9. FULL NP1.0 ACQUISITION & STREAMING PIPELINE (beyond IMRO)#

This branch (feature/NPX1.0ProbeSupport) added the entire NP1.0 path; sections 3-6 cover the IMRO map itself. The rest of the 1.0 support, for the record:

9.1 PROBE DETECTION + INIT (NpInterface.cpp ~964-1196)#

  • detectProbe/discoverProbeHardware: np_getProbeHardwareID -> ProductNumber; "PRB_1" or "NP1" => ProbeType::eNpx1 (getProbeProperties() -> {supported, "Neuropixels 1.0"}).
  • NP1 init: np_writeProbeConfiguration + np_bistSR (shift-register self-test) + np_setOPMODE(RECORDING); loads ADC + gain calibration CSVs; parses the user IMRO file or loadDefaultImro(); an initial applyImroToHardware() before the config lock. CALIBRATION (2026-06-26): the per-probe ADC + gain CSVs are VENDORED locally at /CalibrationInfo (copied from the OpenEphys install, deployed next to the exe by POST_BUILD). NpInterface.cpp resolves them via QCoreApplication::applicationDirPath()/../CalibrationInfo//_{ADCCalibration,gainCalValues}.csv -- the same convention as config/. Was a hardcoded OpenEphys absolute path. UPDATE (2026-06-26, per user): the OpenEphys FALLBACK was REMOVED (it only ever resolved on one machine). If a probe's local CSVs are missing, NpInterface.cpp now qCritical-logs AND emits IDataSource::sigGuiStatus, which WebSocketServer relays as a {"event":"guiStatus","kind":"error"} message -> the red toolbar status in the GUI (NetworkClient -> window.guiStatus). The emit is DEFERRED (QMetaObject::invokeMethod queued) so it lands after onSourceSelection has registered the source + connected sigGuiStatus. sigGuiStatus is a new, reusable C++ source -> GUI alert channel on IDataSource (parallels the recorder's recordingStatus relay).

9.2 ACQUISITION WORKER -- Npx1Probe (NpInterface.cpp ~247-379)#

  • startAcq() spawns a QThread + Npx1Probe worker (HighPriority); a Qt::PreciseTimer at 1 ms drives slDoAcquireDataLoop; np_setSWTrigger(mSlot) starts hardware streaming. State -> eStreaming.
  • Each tick: np_readElectrodeData into a PER-INSTANCE scratch (mSuperframes, up to 64 superframes; was a static local -> made per-instance for safety). NP1 superframe = 12 AP timestamps + apData[12][384] + lfpData[384] + Status[12].
  • DEMUX: per superframe, write 1 LFP sample (384 ch) to the LFP buffer and 12 AP samples (384 ch each) to the AP buffer, each with PacketInfo (Timestamp/Status); commitWrite(producerId, 12) AP and (...,1) LFP per superframe; write indices advanced modulo capacity (guards mid-copy wraparound).
  • stopAcq(): emit sigNpsRequestDaqStop -> worker stops the timer; thread quit + wait(2s); np_arm(mSlot) flushes the hardware buffer; worker/thread pointers nulled (Qt deletes via finished()).

9.3 DUAL STREAMS + DISPLAY#

  • NP1 produces TWO buffers: data.npx.ap (30 kHz) and data.npx.lfp (2.5 kHz), both CircularBuffer. (NP2 uses a single data.npx.raw.)
  • proc.npx.display (DisplayProcessor) consumes each and emits per-pixel min/max-decimated batches to the browser; proc.npx.recorder records them (Task 2).

9.4 GAIN / REFERENCE / FILTER MAPPING (applyImroToHardware + getGainIndex)#

  • getGainIndex: gain VALUE -> API index {50:0,125:1,250:2,500:3,1000:4,1500:5,2000:6,3000:7} (default 500 -> 3).
  • refId -> API: 0=EXT_REF, 1=TIP_REF, 2/3/4=INT_REF with refBank 0/1/2. apFilter: np_setAPCornerFrequency(..., disable_high_pass = (apFilter == 0)).
  • Channel 191 (kNp1ReservedChannel): np_selectElectrode SKIPPED (internal-ref sites 191/575/959); gain/ref/filter still applied. Diagnostics log per-bank tallies {A/B/C} + select/gain/ref/filter fail counts after each apply.

9.5 WIRING (Utils.cpp registerBuiltInTypes; config/Npx1Pxi.yaml; main.cpp)#

  • Registered for NP1: data.npx.ap, data.npx.lfp, interp.npx.ap, interp.npx.lfp, proc.npx.display, proc.npx.recorder; source.npx.pxi (NPX_HW_ENABLED) constructed with slot=3, port=4, dock=1 (HARDCODED -- should scan the basestation).
  • config/Npx1Pxi.yaml (name "NPX 1.0"): source.npx.pxi outputs ap+lfp; proc.npx.display inputs ap+lfp; proc.npx.recorder records ap+lfp (output_filename npx_ap_recording / npx_lfp_recording).
  • main.cpp: registerBuiltInTypes(); setQuitOnLastWindowClosed(false); parseAllConfigs(.yaml/.yml); HTTP :8080 static server + WebSocket :1234.

9.6 KNOWN NP1.0 LIMITATIONS / TODOs (not blocking task 1)#

  • slot/port/dock hardcoded (3/4/1); single probe only (initBasestation stops at the first); np_writeProbeConfiguration read_check=true may be too strict (flip to false if firmware rejects a valid map); NUM_CHANNELS hardcoded 384 on both protocol sides. (RESOLVED 2026-06-26 -- the calibration-CSV-path item that used to live here is gone: the ADC + gain CSVs are now vendored per-probe under /CalibrationInfo and resolved relative to the exe, OpenEphys fallback removed. See 9.1. CalibrationInfo/ is intentionally git-untracked -- see S13.)

Full file:line inventory of the above was captured by an exploration pass; the canonical source is the code in src/modules/neuropixels/. See also images/architecture_overview.md for how this fits the system.

11. PLANNED ARCHITECTURE / FUTURE WORK (decided 2026-06-25; NOT yet implemented -- current code is unchanged. These are the agreed target designs; build them later.)#

11.1 GENERIC, PLUGGABLE RecordProcessor -- "record whatever, given a data interpreter"#

  • PROBLEM: RecordProcessor is effectively single-purpose today. Although it already delegates the byte layout to an IDataInterpreter, the interface + the processor bake in NPX/flat-stream assumptions: RecordProcessor itself owns the QFile + QDataStream and the [uint32 ts][uint16 sync][int16 x 384] 774-byte shape lives in NpxDataInterpreter::serializeAvailableData(QDataStream&, QVector filter). That fits NPX (and would fit NIDAQ analog), but NOT a camera (variable-size frames -> a video container + a timestamp sidecar, which a single flat QDataStream cannot express).
  • GOAL: RecordProcessor records ANY module's data -- NPX AP/LFP now; NIDAQ auxiliary inputs, a camera module, or anything else later -- driven entirely by a per-module IDataInterpreter that knows that module's data shape AND its on-disk format. RecordProcessor stays format-agnostic ("logs whatever").

  • THE PATTERN TO MIMIC = NeuropixelsSource's multi-probe design (S9.1-9.2): NeuropixelsSource (one IDataSource) detects ProbeType and drives a POLYMORPHIC IDataSourceWorker (Npx1Probe / Npx2MSProbe), each worker knowing exactly one probe's acquisition. The recording analog: RecordProcessor (one IProcessor) drives a POLYMORPHIC IDataInterpreter per configured recording (NpxDataInterpreter / NidaqDataInterpreter / CameraDataInterpreter), each knowing exactly one module's serialization. The interpreter is to RecordProcessor what the probe worker is to the source.

  • THE EXACT LAYERED STRUCTURE (three layers): LAYER 1 -- RecordProcessor (generic orchestrator; format-agnostic). Owns ONLY: the worker thread + poll timer, the list of active recordings, and the Start/Stop lifecycle (setRecording). On Start -> tell each interpreter to begin; each tick -> tell each to flush; on Stop -> tell each to end. Knows NOTHING about channel counts, sample widths, file layouts, or stream types. (Moves the QFile/QDataStream/774-byte ownership it has today DOWN to Layer 2.) LAYER 2 -- IDataInterpreter subtype (per-module; the pluggable format+SINK layer; the analog of the per-probe worker). One concrete class per data kind, shipped by the owning module and registered by name "interp..". Each one OWNS ITS OWN OUTPUT TARGET, so a recording can be a single binary file, OR a binary + a .meta sidecar, OR a video container + a timestamp index -- whatever that data needs. Knows: which registry data object/CircularBuffer it reads, the sample shape, the serialization, and the metadata header. LAYER 3 -- the data object (CircularBuffer or other) the source produced. Unchanged; the interpreter binds to it by registry name in configure(), exactly as today.

  • REQUIRED INTERFACE CHANGE (IDataInterpreter): generalize from "write samples into a QDataStream the processor handed me, filtered by a QVector of channel indices" (NPX-flavoured; one flat stream) to an OUTPUT-OWNING lifecycle. Target contract (names illustrative): bool configure(cfg) // bind data object + per-recording config (filename, filter, rate) bool beginRecording(QString basePath)// interpreter OPENS whatever files/containers it needs void writeAvailable() // pull new samples from its buffer and append (replaces // serializeAvailableData(stream, filter)) void endRecording() // flush + close ALL its outputs void updateReadMarkers() // unchanged: advance read index while idle QString describeFormat() [optional] // emit a sidecar header (rate, N, probe, IMRO, dtype) -- // also closes the "no metadata header" gap (Recording.md S5) The QDataStream and the QVector filter become INTERNAL details of interpreters that want them (NPX, NIDAQ analog); a camera interpreter ignores both and writes frames its own way.

  • PER-MODULE INTERPRETERS (examples that motivate this): interp.npx.ap / interp.npx.lfp (EXISTS, NpxDataInterpreter) -> [uint32 ts][uint16 sync][int16 x384] (+ a future .meta sidecar) interp.nidaq.analog / .digital (FUTURE, NidaqDataInterpreter)-> NIDAQ aux inputs in NIDAQ's own layout (timestamp + per-channel f32/uint, its own channel count) interp.camera.frames (FUTURE, CameraDataInterpreter)-> a video container (or per-frame files) PLUS a [frame#][hw-ts][sync] index sidecar so frames align to the neural clock

  • WIRING (already this generic -- no central edit needed): config/.yaml proc..recorder lists "recordings:", each = { data_name, data_interpreter_name, output_filename [, channels, rate] }. To record a new stream: add a recordings: entry naming the new interp.*, and registerDataInterpreter <...>("interp..") in Utils.cpp. RecordProcessor needs NO edit -- same "add a config entry, nothing central to touch" property the source/QWebChannel command side already has.
  • NET: RecordProcessor sheds all NPX specifics; each module ships an interpreter describing how to log its data; recording works for any module that provides one. Until built, behaviour is unchanged (NpxDataInterpreter keeps working as-is). When built, extend Recording.md (format-per-interpreter) and AddingASinkModule.md (how to add an interpreter).

11.2 SINK COMMAND THREADING -- main-thread handle + worker behind it (removes the QWebChannel marshal)#

  • CONTEXT: developer_docs/ModuleCommunication.md S5. QWebChannel invokes Q_INVOKABLEs synchronously on the GUI/transport thread (DirectConnection, so it can return values), NOT the object's own thread. Because ProcessorManager moveToThread's the ENTIRE IProcessor onto a worker thread, a sink's invokable (e.g. RecordProcessor::setRecording) lands on the wrong thread and must marshal back with QMetaObject::invokeMethod(..., Qt::QueuedConnection) -- a wart repeated on every sink invokable.
  • LONG-TERM FIX (the agreed direction): mirror NeuropixelsSource, where the QObject the browser talks to lives on the MAIN thread and only an acquisition WORKER (IDataSourceWorker) lives on its own thread. Apply the same handle/worker split to sinks:
    • The IProcessor "handle" -- config + Q_INVOKABLE command surface + QWebChannel registration -- stays on the MAIN thread.
    • The data loop (timer/poll or blocking consume) runs on a WORKER moved to its own thread (an IProcessorWorker, analogous to IDataSourceWorker).
    • Commands then run on the main thread NATURALLY (no marshal in the invokable) and reach the worker via ordinary queued signals/slots -- the explicit, idiomatic cross-thread hop -- with each piece of shared state owned by exactly one side. COST: a ProcessorManager refactor + splitting each processor into handle+worker (RecordProcessor, DisplayProcessor, RippleDetector). Large, but it removes the footgun for ALL sinks and makes sink commands look exactly like source commands. (This pairs naturally with 11.1 -- do them together: the generic RecordProcessor handle on the main thread, its interpreters/worker doing the writing.)
  • INTERIM (optional, if we want the wart gone sooner): a one-line IProcessor helper runOnSelf(functor) wrapping the QueuedConnection marshal (ModuleCommunication.md S5 option A). Keeps it lock-free, hides the boilerplate. Not a substitute for the split -- just nicer until then.

11.3 GENERIC DISPLAY PROCESSOR -- one decimator paradigm across all sources (decided 2026-06-26)#

  • CONTEXT: as of 2026-06-26 there are TWO display processors: Npx::DisplayProcessor (NPX: int16 / Neuropixels::PacketInfo / hardcoded 384 / AVX2 min/max / NPX gain table + ".ap/.lfp/.raw" name-sniff / timestamp-edge binning by 100kHz ticks) and NiDaq::NiDaqDisplayProcessor (NI-DAQ: double / NiDaqPacketInfo / channel count INFERRED from sampleDimensions() / scalar min/max / configurable per-source scale / sample-count binning). NiDaqDisplayProcessor was deliberately built new+isolated (NiDaq.md decision 2) to avoid destabilizing the working NPX path before the in-vivo test. They now share the exact wire format ([u8 idLen][idStr][u32 numCh][u32 numBins], then per bin [f32 ts][f32 min,max]xN) but DUPLICATE the timer/batch loop, per-stream StreamState, packet (QBuffer/QDataStream) assembly, and the min/max decimation skeleton.
  • DECISION (ultimately): unify them into ONE generic display decimator that every source plugs into the same way -- the same theme as 11.1 (generic RecordProcessor + per-module interpreters) and 11.2 (handle/worker split). Channel count ALWAYS inferred from the source buffer's sampleDimensions(); display scale ALWAYS configurable per source (+ the per-column UI scale). A new modality (camera, etc.) then gets a live display by supplying a small adapter + config -- no bespoke processor class, same "add config, nothing central to edit" property as the recorder/command sides.
  • WHAT TO PARAMETERIZE (the only real differences): sample dtype (int16 vs double) and decimation impl (AVX2 vs scalar); aux/timestamp type + how SECONDS are derived (NPX hardware ticks / kHeadstageSamplingRate vs NI-DAQ sample-index / configured rate); binning key (timestamp-edge vs sample-count); scaling (NPX gain table vs configurable scale). Everything else (timer batching, StreamState, header+packet assembly, numCh-in-header) is already common and can move to a shared base.
  • LIKELY SHAPE: a templated base, e.g. DecimatingDisplayProcessor, or a generic processor with a per-source "sample adapter" (dtype + timestamp->seconds + scale). DisplayProcessor and NiDaqDisplayProcessor become thin adapters. Do this when convenient; until then the two stay separate (the duplication is small and the NPX path must stay rock-solid through the in-vivo work). Reference: developer_docs/NiDaq.md S7 (what shipped) + this entry.

12. FEATURES (12.1 IMPLEMENTED 2026-06-26; 12.2 proposed, awaiting decisions)#

12.1 LIVE NI-DAQ CHANNEL DISPLAY -- developer_docs/NiDaq.md [IMPLEMENTED 2026-06-26, built clean]#

  • Shows NI-DAQ analog channels live in the Live Data tab; a column's stream dropdown selects "nidaq" and all analog channels render in that one column (<=20 visible + slider). Decisions taken: reuse Live Data (A); new isolated NiDaqDisplayProcessor (A); channel count carried in the display packet header; configurable scale (per-source YAML "scale" baked in C++ + per-column "x" multiplier in the UI).
  • SHIPPED: src/modules/nidaq/NiDaqDisplayProcessor.{hpp,cpp} (consumes data.nidaq.raw double buffer, INFERS channel count from sampleDimensions(), sample-count min/max decimation, per-source scale); registered proc.nidaq.display (Utils.cpp + CMakeLists). Wire header now [u8 idLen][idStr][u32 numCh] [u32 numBins] -- NPX DisplayProcessor emits numCh=384 too. WebSocketServer connects ALL display procs (mDisplayProcNames). Browser: NetworkClient reads per-stream numCh, DataStore allocates per-stream, ChartColumn + relayoutByDepth apply probe depth/filters only to probe-width streams (NI-DAQ = sequential), per-column scale input. config/Npx1PxiNiDaq.yaml + NpxPxiNiDaq.yaml gained proc.nidaq.display.
  • POST-TEST FIXES (2026-06-26, after the user's first hardware run; all built clean + deployed):
    • BLANK NI-DAQ DISPLAY (bug): updateAppendModes() only marked PROBE columns, so with an NP1 map applied + probe columns in 'B' (interleaved) mode, appendPacked=false -- but a NI-DAQ column renders the PACKED series, so it was never appended -> blank. Fix: a column showing a non-probe-width stream now forces needPacked; and the stream-selector onchange calls updateAppendModes (it didn't before). (webinterface/main.js, ChartColumn.js)
    • NI-DAQ ACQUISITION OVERFLOW -200279 "not able to keep up" (bug, on the 2nd play): NIDAQSource::startAcq re-connected QThread::started->slStartAcquiringData every play WITHOUT Qt::UniqueConnection, so play #2 double-started the DAQmx tasks; AND slStopAcquiringData never stopped the tasks, so the hardware FIFO overflowed while paused. Fix: Qt::UniqueConnection on the three startAcq connects; DAQmxStopTask (AI+DI) on pause for a clean Start->Stop->Start; deleteLater the previously-leaked timer. (NiDaqInterface.cpp)
  • DISPLAY REFINEMENTS (2026-06-26, per user; frontend-only):
    • DEFAULT 2-COLUMN SPLIT: when both a probe stream and the NI-DAQ stream exist, column 0 defaults to the probe and column 1 to NI-DAQ (AppController.applyDefaultColumnStreams); a manual stream pick (userPickedStream) wins. NPX-only profiles keep both columns on the probe.
    • CONTEXT-AWARE PER-COLUMN SCALE: the scale control now adapts to the column's stream -- PROBE streams show a MICROVOLT RANGE (peak-to-peak per trace, label "µV"; smaller = zoomed in; zoom = SLOT*100/range, where Config.MICROVOLTS_PER_DISPLAY_UNIT=100 matches the NP1 DisplayProcessor scaleFactor), while NI-DAQ shows a volts "×V" multiplier. (webinterface/ChartColumn.js, Config.js)
  • NOT yet hardware-verified (built + JS-validated + deployed). See NiDaq.md S7.

12.2 PROBE ACTIVITY HEATMAP -- developer_docs/ProbeActivityHeatmap.md#

  • After the map is applied and data streams, color the SELECTED electrodes by live activity (OpenEphys ProbeViewer style, dark-mode SciChart) instead of freezing the probe tab. Two shortcuts make it cheap: (1) the metric is peak-to-peak = max-min, which DisplayProcessor ALREADY streams per channel -> derive it in JS with no backend change (OpenEphys uses the same p2p over a ~100 ms window @ 10 Hz, Inferno colormap, fixed clim default 500 uV, grey for no-data); (2) SciChart v5.2.35 in our bundle natively has PaletteProvider / UniformHeatmapDataSeries / HeatmapColorMap / GradientColorPalette (verified), so no canvas hack -- recolor the existing electrode scatter via a per-point PaletteProvider keyed by window.imroLiveView.electrodeOf(ch). Effort ~2-3 days frontend-only (Option A/A). Decisions pending: JS-derived vs C++ CAR+bandpass activity, PaletteProvider recolor vs UniformHeatmap panel, colormap, clim/units, "blink" = intensity vs literal pulse, auto Activity-Mode vs toggle, NP1 now / NP2 later.

13. PLATFORM -- WINDOWS HARDWARE BOX -> LINUX / SIM DEV (2026-07-06)#

13.1 THE MOVE#

  • Through 2026-07-03 all dev + testing ran on the WINDOWS box with LIVE NPX 1.0 (PXI) + NI-DAQ hardware physically attached (every "test1..test5" recording above is real hardware). On 2026-07-06 the rig went to a labmate for recording, and dev moved to a LINUX machine with NO Neuropixels / NI-DAQ hardware.
  • CMakeLists.txt is already cross-platform: if(WIN32) defines NPX_HW_ENABLED / NIDAQ_HW_ENABLED and pulls in the hardware sources (source.npx.pxi, source.nidaq); elseif(Linux) leaves them OFF and compiles the NeuropixAPI STUBS. SimDataSource + SynthRippleSource are always built. So a Linux build produces a hardware-free binary; the sim source is the primary data path there.
  • Docs updated for the move (2026-07-06): developer_docs/README.md (Linux build & run section), ControlCLI.md (Linux headless launch = just ./build/bin/SpikesPeak), RecordingTest.md (platform banner -- the hardware runbook is Windows-only; the Linux/sim equivalent is the SimDataLinux plan).

13.2 CURRENT TASK -- SIMULATED NP1.0 SOURCE FULLY WORKING ON LINUX (APPROVED 2026-07-06; not started)#

  • Goal: build + stream (through the REAL display pipeline, like a probe) + record, so the headless spikespeak_cli.py "Recording Test" runs with zero hardware. Full spec + phased plan: developer_docs/SimDataLinux.md (APPROVED -- has a "For the new Linux agent -- START HERE" block).
  • Why it doesn't work today: SimDataSource emits the OLD wire format (no stream-id header) and is SPECIAL-CASED in WebSocketServer (bypasses the display processors), and config/NpxSimData.yaml is source-only -- so sim currently renders nothing. Fix (Option A): sim writes raw NP1.0 into data.npx.ap / data.npx.lfp and rides the normal proc.npx.display / proc.lfp / proc.npx.recorder pipeline; then de-special-case WebSocketServer.
  • DECISIONS LOCKED (2026-07-06, user): (1) scope = STREAM + RECORD, as identical to a real probe as possible (Option A). (2) depth sorting = PROBE-LIKE -- sim publishes a synthetic default Bank-0 IMRO map (not the sequential fallback). (3) CalibrationInfo/ stays untracked (see 13.3). (4) *** CORRECTED 2026-07-06 (see S14): the original "one FLEXIBLE, HARDWARE-AGNOSTIC sim source with a polymorphic device profile" is WRONG. Each SOURCE MODULE gets its OWN simulated source class (data shape is hardware-specific: NPX int16 AP/LFP vs NI-DAQ double+digital vs camera frames). Flexibility = the scenario/signal WITHIN a module's sim, selected by config profiles / a "what to simulate" picker -- NOT one class emulating different hardware. *** (5) FOLD source.npx.synthripples into the same Option-A treatment -- software-simulated ripples (easier than hardware-injected) that stream + record through the real pipeline; needed for the upcoming saline audio-file playback test (probe in saline) before in vivo. Full detail: developer_docs/SimDataLinux.md.

13.3 DECISION -- CalibrationInfo/ STAYS GIT-UNTRACKED (2026-07-06, user)#

  • The per-probe ADC + gain calibration CSVs under /CalibrationInfo (vendored 2026-06-26, see 9.1) are DEVICE-SPECIFIC: every lab has different probes/serials, so these files are per-user, not shared source. DECISION: CalibrationInfo/ remains .gitignored / untracked -- it is NOT committed to the repo. Each user supplies their own probe's calibration folder (copied from their OpenEphys install, keyed by serial).
  • CONSEQUENCE for the build: the POST_BUILD "copy CalibrationInfo/ next to the exe" step must become CONDITIONAL (if(EXISTS ...)) so a fresh clone WITHOUT that folder (e.g. the Linux sim box) still builds. This is folded into the SimDataLinux Phase 0 work. Sim needs no calibration, so a missing folder is fine there; on a real hardware box the user drops their CalibrationInfo/ in before building.

13.4 DONE -- SIMULATED NP1.0 SOURCE FULLY WORKING ON LINUX (2026-07-06; built + verified hardware-free)#

Implemented SimDataLinux.md Option A end-to-end on the Linux/no-hardware box. select "Simulated Data" now behaves like a real 1.0 probe: it builds, streams through the REAL display pipeline, and records. Verified hardware-free with the control CLI + numpy (matplotlib/pip unavailable on this box, so the plot_recording.py PNGs were replaced by a numpy check -- stronger than eyeballing).

  • PHASE 0 (green build): (1) The vendored external/neuropixels/api/include/v4/NeuropixAPI.h had UNGUARDED MSVC macros (#define NP_EXPORT __declspec(dllexport) / NP_APIC __stdcall) -> GCC hard error. Guarded both with #ifndef _WIN32 (empty on non-Windows), mirroring the ALREADY-patched v3 header. Nobody had actually compiled the Linux branch before (bin/ was empty) -- this was the real blocker, not the stubs. (2) Made the CalibrationInfo/ POST_BUILD copy + install CONDITIONAL (if(EXISTS ...), decision #3 / S13.3). Build is now green on Linux (Qt 6.11.1 gcc_64, cmake/ninja from ~/Qt/Tools). Launches headless with QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen ./bin/SpikesPeak --no-browser, serves HTTP:8080 + WS:1234; the CLI drives it.
  • PHASE 1 (sim = real NP1.0 data): REWROTE SimDataSource.{hpp,cpp}. It no longer emits the old display packet; instead a worker (SimNpx1Worker, on its own QThread like Npx1Probe) writes raw int16 into data.npx.ap (30 kHz) + data.npx.lfp (2.5 kHz) as 12-AP+1-LFP superframes with a reconstructed PacketInfo (100 kHz-clock timestamp + a 1 Hz square SYNC pulse on Status bit 6 = the recorder's Status>>6). Signal model: a PURELY software-generated clean sine, IDENTICAL on every one of the 384 channels (AP 10 Hz, LFP 4 Hz, ~150 ADC counts) -- deliberately NO per-channel variation / no probe or damaged-amp modelling (the synthetic source is just sine waves / software ripples, not a stand-in for any real probe). Publishes a default Bank-0 IMRO map + the full IMRO Q_INVOKABLE surface (getImroAsJson / updateImroFromJson / applyMapToHardware / getImroAsImroText / loadImroFromText) + applyNamedMap (default/bank0/1/2) so the live view depth-sorts BYTE-IDENTICALLY to hardware. Self-contained (does NOT touch the hardware-only NpInterface.hpp, which is compiled out on Linux); NP1/384 assumptions sit behind a small SimSignalProfile seam (decision #4 -- a future NP2/NeuroNexus profile slots in without pipeline edits).
  • PHASE 2 (config + de-special-case): config/NpxSimData.yaml now wires source.npx.simdata ap+lfp -> proc.npx.display + proc.npx.recorder (records electrodes [1,192,196]). REMOVED the "Simulated Data" special-casing in WebSocketServer::{onSourceSelection,setPlaying} -- sim now flows through onSourceSelection -> display procs -> broadcastData like every other source. Broadened the frontend gate in NetworkClient.js (was selectedSource.includes("NPX")) to bind the QWebChannel/IMRO panel for ANY ready source that registers an npx object, so "Simulated Data" gets the full probe panel + recorders. Emits a deferred guiStatus "Found simulated Neuropixels 1.0 Probe" so waitready works.
  • DECISION #5 (synthripples): config/NpxSynthRipples.yaml gained a proc.npx.recorder so source.npx.synthripples STREAMS + RECORDS through the real pipeline (it already fed proc.npx.display + proc.lfp -> proc.rippledetector). Verified: 14 s recording written AND the ripple detector fired.
  • VERIFICATION (all on Linux, no hardware): CLI select "Simulated Data" -> waitready -> probemap default -> play -> record -> stop -> pause -> disconnect -> quit produced ap+lfp .bin + sidecars; loaded back by spikespeak_postprocess: AP = clean 10 Hz sine IDENTICAL on all recorded channels (pp 708 / sigma 249 uV), LFP = clean 4 Hz sine identical on all (pp 1406 / sigma 497 uV); sync field = clean 1 Hz square (8 rising edges / 8.4 s, monotonic ts). plot_recording.py channels.png/sync.png render (needs conda base -- see §13.5). A live WS client saw the display broadcasting BOTH ap+lfp streams (384 ch, 20 bins). Synthripples: 14 s raw recording + 3 ripple detections. Tests kept < 30 s as consecutive RecordingTests// folders.
  • FILES: external/neuropixels/api/include/v4/NeuropixAPI.h (macro guards), CMakeLists.txt (conditional CalibrationInfo), src/modules/neuropixels/SimDataSource.{hpp,cpp} (rewrite), src/core/WebSocketServer.cpp (de-special-case), webinterface/NetworkClient.js (bind gate), config/NpxSimData.yaml + config/NpxSynthRipples.yaml. NOT yet committed (commit only when asked).
  • DONE: NP1.0 GUI record-channel selection UI (parts 4+5, S10) -- built + verified 2026-07-06, see S13.7. STILL OPEN: simulated NI-DAQ source + the NI-DAQ record-selection tab + a "what to simulate" picker when selecting Simulated Data (planned 2026-07-06; see S14).

13.6 DEDICATED BROWSER APP-WINDOW ON LAUNCH (2026-07-06, user request; built + verified)#

  • Running the executable interactively now opens the UI in a DEDICATED browser app-window (a separate browser session in its own chromeless window) instead of a new tab in the user's normal browser -- the user's preferred UX. main.cpp launchDedicatedBrowser() finds a Chromium-family browser (brave-browser / chrome / chromium / edge -- Windows probes known install paths, Linux/mac use PATH) and launches --app=http://localhost:8080 --user-data-dir=~/.spikespeak/browser-profile --no-first-run --no-default-browser-check --window-size=1600,1000 detached. The isolated persistent profile keeps it separate from normal browsing and remembers geometry. Closing the window drops the last WS client -> the existing grace-timer shuts the backend down (window = app lifetime).
  • Flags: --no-browser (headless, unchanged), --tab/--system-browser (legacy new-tab via QDesktopServices::openUrl). If NO Chromium-family browser is found, it falls back to a tab automatically.
  • Verified on Linux/:0: ./bin/SpikesPeak (no flags) opened a chromeless Brave app window titled "SpikesPeak" via /usr/bin/brave-browser showing the UI (Source: None, Ready); clean teardown.

VERIFIED: probe subset + NI-DAQ [0,3,5] -> AP/LFP got the probe channels, the NI-DAQ .bin got exactly [0,3,5] (sidecar + postprocess). => Parts 4+5 fully complete (NPX + NI-DAQ). - ✅ RESOLVED 2026-07-07 (was DEFERRED): unify sine+ripple into ONE NP1.0 sim class with a user-selectable scenario. SimulatedDataSource now reads inject_ripples (+ tunable amp/freq/period/noise) from its YAML node; config/SimNpx1Ripples.yaml is the ripple scenario (sine vs ripple purely by config, same sim class), and the ripple detector FIRES on it by reading the native data.npx.lfp band DIRECTLY (int16, 2.5 kHz, decimation 1 -- no proc.lfp; the AP band is high-passed on real hardware so it can't carry ripples). The NP2-style h5 SynthRippleSource is no longer the stand-in for NP1 ripples. See §20* for the full write-up + verification.

Original plan detail (as approved) below.

  • BUILD FACT that shapes this: the ENTIRE nidaq module is Windows-only today -- src/modules/CMakeLists.txt only add_subdirectory(nidaq) under NIDAQ_HW_ENABLED, and Utils.cpp registers data.nidaq.raw / interp.nidaq.raw / proc.nidaq.display / proc.nidaq.recorder inside #ifdef NIDAQ_HW_ENABLED. So on Linux there is NO NI-DAQ pipeline at all. BUT the data types + interpreter + display are LIB-FREE (NiDaq.hpp does NOT include NIDAQmx.h); only NiDaqInterface.cpp (the real DAQmx source) needs the lib. Fix mirrors the neuropixels module exactly (always-compile sim/interpreter/display; gate the hardware .cpp).
  • NI-DAQ + LINUX: NIDAQmx.h IS cross-platform (has linux/APPLE branches), so NI-DAQmx can run on Linux with NI's Linux runtime -- but the vendored lib is Windows-only (external/nidaq/api/lib64/NIDAQMX.lib, no .so). Real NI-DAQ HW on Linux is out of scope (would need libnidaqmx.so + linking). The SIM needs none of it.

PLAN (parts): 1. Un-gate the LIB-FREE NI-DAQ pipeline so it compiles + registers on Linux: split nidaq/CMakeLists.txt (always compile NiDaq.{hpp,cpp} + NiDaqDisplayProcessor + the new sim source; gate NiDaqInterface.cpp under NIDAQ_HW_ENABLED); add_subdirectory(nidaq) unconditionally; move the data.nidaq.raw / interp.nidaq.raw / proc.nidaq.display / proc.nidaq.recorder registrations OUT of the NIDAQ_HW_ENABLED guard (keep only source.nidaq gated). 2. NEW simulated NI-DAQ source (its own class) NiDaqSimSource (+ worker), registered source.nidaq.sim, always compiled. Writes double samples + NiDaqPacketInfo{timestamp,digitalBitmask,samplesInBlock} into data.nidaq.raw, replicating a real device: N analog ch (default 8) @ 40 kHz, float64, realistic V range; ch0 = a shared TTL sync train aligned to the NPX sim sync field (for the NPX<->NI-DAQ sync test), optionally with a small deliberate drift (~10 ppm) so synchronize() recovers it; other ch = analog sines + one quiet "floating" ch; a toggling digital line in the bitmask. 3. "What to simulate" PICKER: selecting "Simulated Data" prompts for the scenario (NPX1.0 sine / NPX1.0 ripples / NPX1.0 + NI-DAQ), mapping to test config YAMLs. Ripple scenario: reuse SimNpx1Worker's inject_ripples profile flag (one NPX sim class, sine-vs-ripples via config) rather than the separate NP2-style h5 SynthRippleSource. New test configs: SimNpx1.yaml, SimNpx1Ripples.yaml, SimNpx1NiDaq.yaml. 4. NI-DAQ record-selection TAB (finishes parts 4+5): a channel grid with Record-all/Unselect/Set committing to proc.nidaq.recorder. Needs per-recorder selections (window.recordChannels becomes {npx:[...],nidaq:[...]}), since NPX and NI-DAQ are separate recorders in window.activeRecorders.

OPEN DECISIONS (approve before build): (a) sim NI-DAQ signal model params (ch count, rate, V range, which ch is sync, drift amount); (b) ripple scenario = reuse inject_ripples flag [rec] vs keep separate SynthRippleSource; (c) picker UX = modal on selecting Simulated Data [rec] vs separate dropdown entries; (d) picker discovery = a sim_variant: YAML key [rec] vs hardcoded frontend list; (e) test-config names + whether to rename NpxSimData.yaml; (f) build order (user suggested the picker "beforehand").

14.1 FIXES (2026-07-06, post-review)#

  • QUIT HANG / "QProcess::Crashed" (fixed). With a REAL console attached (e.g. run from Qt Creator's terminal), a CLI/GUI quit HUNG the process: KeyboardListener runs a blocking std::getline on a worker thread that QCoreApplication::quit() can't interrupt, so the process never exited and Qt Creator SIGTERM-killed it (code 15, "QProcess::Crashed"). Headless (stdin closed) was fine -- getline returns and the thread ends. FIX (main.cpp): on QCoreApplication::aboutToQuit, terminate() + wait() the kbListener thread (it only holds a stdin read; safe at shutdown). Reproduced via a pty: was hang -> now clean exit code 0.
  • QObject::startTimer: ... dispatcher destroyed warning on quit (fixed too). It printed during ~WebSocketServer because that ran during ~QApplication after the event dispatcher was gone (the grace QTimer / socket teardown touched a timer). FIX (main.cpp): delete the WebSocketServer in a second aboutToQuit handler, while the dispatcher is still alive. Verified via a GUI-mode (real display) + pty repro: shutdown log is now CLEAN (0 startTimer warnings, exit 0).
  • NI-DAQ record grid now 1-INDEXED (fixed). The grid showed 0-indexed channels while the live view shows 1-indexed (CH 1..8, ChartColumn.js ${ch+1}). The grid buttons now display ch+1 to match; the stored/committed/recorded index stays 0-based (recorder convention). Verified: displayed "1,4,6" -> recorded read-out channels [0,3,5].
  • NI-DAQ record section MOVED below the probe (layout). It was sandwiched between the probe instructions and the probe scatter. Now it's a sibling AFTER #scichart-imro-root (with a separator + its own <h3>), so all probe things (controls + scatter) group together and NI-DAQ sits separately below. setProbeMode('edit') + the connect-reset now also hide #nidaq-record-section.

Reference: OpenEphys-NP2-Reference.md.

15.1 The five pieces (all DONE)#

  1. HARDWARE default map (Gap 1) -- NpInterface.cpp loadDefaultImro() NP2 branch builds the 384-ch default (shank 0, Bank A, electrodes 0-383, each channel's natural electrode via the per-shank block-permutation lookup). Uses a NEW shared helper Np2::electrodeToChannel/bankOf in Npx.hpp (the exact OpenEphys Geometry.cpp permutation tables {0,2,4,6,5,7,1,3} etc). initBasestation's NP2 branch now LOADS that map (parse user .imro or default) -- it previously only set references and left the map EMPTY.
  2. HARDWARE apply (Gap 2) -- applyImroToHardware() NP2 branch: DISCONNECT all 4 shank switches (np_setReference(...,NONE_REF,...)) ONCE, then per channel np_selectElectrode(...,shank,bank) + np_setReference(...,shank,refId,0) (Ext=own shank; 1-4=Tip shank k-1). NO gain/AP-filter (fixed-gain probe). Mirrors OpenEphys/Probes/Neuropixels2.cpp selectElectrodes/setAllReferences/writeConfiguration. The existing applyMapToHardware read-check verify then applies unchanged.
  3. SIMULATED 2.0 source (own class) -- NEW SimData2Source.{hpp,cpp} -> class SimulatedData2Source, registered source.npx.sim2 (always compiled; modelled on SimDataSource + SynthRippleSource). Writes int16 into the SINGLE data.npx.raw (30 kHz; NP2 has NO ap/lfp split) with a reconstructed PacketInfo (100 kHz- clock timestamp + a 1 Hz square sync on Status bit 6). Signal = a clean sine with a fast (10 Hz, AP-like) + slow (4 Hz, LFP-like) component, IDENTICAL on all channels; combined amplitude (~120 ADC) matches the NP1 sim's proven display magnitude at the raw scaleFactor. Publishes a default NP2 IMRO map (shank/electrodeId) + the full IMRO Q_INVOKABLE surface (getImroAsJson/updateImroFromJson/applyMapToHardware/loadImroFromText) + applyNamedMap("default"). "2.0 + LFP": proc.lfp DERIVES data.lfp from data.npx.raw (same wiring as NpxSynthRipples). New configs SimNpx2.yaml + SimNpx2NiDaq.yaml, both tagged sim_variant: so they join the "Simulated Data" picker automatically (ConfigManager::getSimVariant is already generic).
  4. RECORD-CHANNEL SELECTION for 2.0 (Gap 3 / parts 4+5) -- ImroManager.js: the renderElectrodeMap NP2 else-branch is now a full INTERACTIVE 4-shank editor (was a static placeholder): 4 shanks side by side, click an electrode -> route its channel to it, drag a vertical box on one shank -> assign a run, Shank dropdown + Bank A-D presets (applyNp2ShankBank), electrode labels on active dots, auto-zoom. Record mode (renderRecordSelection + appliedRecordInfo + the mouseup branch) extended to the 4-shank geometry (electrode = electrodeId, shank-aware; NO ch191 reference exclusion -- NP2 has no reserved channel). A JS NP2_electrodeToChannel mirrors the C++ helper exactly (node self-test: known values vs OpenEphys + bijection for all 16 shank/bank combos PASS). window.recordChannels = {npx:[...],nidaq:[...]} carries the NP2 read-out channels; main.js routing is probe-agnostic. index.html gained #np2-quick-formats.
  5. RECORDING -- the recorder is probe-agnostic (interp.npx.raw records data.npx.raw); confirmed the .bin.json sidecar + spikespeak_postprocess read it. The NI-DAQ record grid + sync test carry over.

15.2 A GENERAL live-view fix this surfaced (single-stream probes)#

main.js handleNewStream called relayoutByDepth() (which runs updateAppendModes()) BEFORE refreshStreamSelectors() assigned each column its currentStreamId, so updateAppendModes saw probeStream = false and defaulted to the PACKED series. NP1's TWO streams (ap+lfp) hid this -- the 2nd stream's handleNewStream re-ran it with currentStreamId already set. NP2's SINGLE raw stream never got that second pass, so an L+R (interleaved) column's series was never appended and the live view rendered BLANK (data was flowing, just to the wrong series). FIX: handleNewStream now calls updateAppendModes() AFTER refreshStreamSelectors(). This fixes the NP2 live view AND hardens the append-mode logic for any future single-stream source.

17. NP2.0 MAPPING VERIFIED vs THE OFFICIAL MANUAL + TWO GUI FIXES (2026-07-07)#

Prompted by a user review of Neuropixels_2-0_User_Manual_V1-0-6/ (Excel + pp.40-45).

  • Channel↔electrode mapping VERIFIED CORRECT. Cross-checked Np2::electrodeToChannel / NP2_electrodeToChannel against all 5,120 entries of Neuropix_2_0_Electrode-Channel-mapping.xlsx (4 shanks × 4 banks): 0 mismatches. The per-shank block permutation matches Figure 37 exactly (bottom→top: shank0 0,2,4,6,5,7,1,3, shank1 1,3,5,7,4,6,0,2, shank2 4,6,0,2,1,3,5,7, shank3 5,7,1,3,0,2,4,6, channel-group 0 at the tip). Electrode geometry (row=e>>1, col=e&1, El0=tip/bottom, even=left) matches Figure 35 + OpenEphys Geometry.cpp. KEY DISTINCTION (documented for future readers): the electrode number is a physical site (row=e//2 = depth); the channel number is the amplifier/read-out index (transmission order) with NO physical meaning — the .xlsx maps channel→electrode and is deliberately scrambled, which is why a channel-ordered electrode list looks non-monotonic. Streaming/recording is in channel order; the depth view re-sorts by each channel's electrode depth.
  • FIX 1 — shank labels now 0-indexed (0-3), matching the manual / .imro / hardware. The NP2 editor (index.html #np2-shank-select + hint, ImroManager.js applyNp2ShankBank status) was 1-indexed (1-4), which was INCONSISTENT with the live-view shank dropdown (ChartColumn.js already showed Shank ${sh} = 0-3). Now all UI shows shank 0-3. Verified in-browser: editor dropdown 0/1/2/3, "shanks shown left→right as 0,1,2,3", "Shank 2 Bank C" preset lands on the 3rd physical shank.
  • FIX 2 — probe-type mismatch dialog (no more silent wrong-probe). Selecting e.g. "NPX 2.0 Multishank" when a 1.0 probe is connected used to silently stream as 1.0. Now NeuropixelsSource::initDataSource infers the profile's EXPECTED probe from its output data (data.npx.raw→NP2, .ap/.lfp→NP1) and, if the DETECTED probe differs, emits a deferred sigGuiStatus(msg,"probe-mismatch"); the frontend (main.js showProbeMismatch) shows a blocking modal with Disconnect (reuses the toolbar disconnect flow) / Continue anyway. Non-fatal (the source still opens the detected probe so nothing crashes). Verified on the real 1.0 rig: selecting "NPX 2.0 Multishank" popped the modal ("configured for a NPX 2.0 Multi-Shank … but a NPX 1.0 probe is connected"); Disconnect tore the source down (→ None).
  • Files: webinterface/index.html, webinterface/ImroManager.js, webinterface/main.js, src/modules/neuropixels/NpInterface.cpp. Rebuilt clean (exit 0), webinterface redeployed. NOT committed.
  • STILL OPEN (unchanged): real-2.0 HARDWARE apply/record (no 2.0 probe on the box yet — §15.4).

18. NP2.0 REFINEMENTS ROUND 2 + FILE ORG + DOCS (2026-07-07, built + verified in-browser)#

More user-review follow-ups on top of §17, then knowledge-transfer housekeeping.

  • Bank labels now 0-3 (were A-D). The manual, the .xlsx, and the .imro/hardware use bank 0-3; the UI showed OpenEphys-style A-D. Renamed the NP2 bank buttons + hint (index.html) and the applyNp2ShankBank status (ImroManager.js) to 0-3. (Internally the bank field was always numeric 0-3; this is display-only.) The 4 banks = the four 384-electrode depth ranges per shank (bank b = electrodes [b×384 … b×384+384); bank 3 is partial, 128 electrodes) — the WITHIN-bank channel↔electrode is the scramble (Fig 37), the banks themselves are ascending contiguous ranges.
  • NP2 .imro / map VALIDATION (Np2::isConsistentEntry in Npx.hpp). A (channel,shank,bank,electrode) entry is only accepted if channel == Np2::electrodeToChannel(shank,electrode) (the FIXED silicon wiring), bank == electrode/384, all values in range, and no read-out channel is reused. Enforced in: NpInterface.cpp parseImroContent (real .imro load) + updateImroFromJson (editor commit), and SimData2Source.cpp loadImroFromText + updateImroFromJson. So a hand-edited / wrong-probe / corrupt NP2 .imro is REJECTED (frontend shows the load-error), never silently loaded as an unrealisable map. Verified in-browser: default map accepted; a tampered map (channel≠lookup) and an impossible .imro rejected; a consistent 2-entry .imro loaded.
  • Click constraints CONFIRMED (no code change needed). Record-mode selection already restricts to the APPLIED map's electrodes (renderRecordSelection draws only applied; the mouseup uses info.keyToChannel.get(...) and ignores non-applied clicks) — you can only pick the streamable/recordable subset. Edit-mode clicks assign a channel via np2Assign = NP2_electrodeToChannel(...), which always yields a valid map (each of the 384 channels reads exactly one electrode). So no path produces an unrealisable map.
  • NP2 per-shank RECTANGLES + TIPS (like NP1). ImroManager.js now draws a shaft BoxAnnotation + an SVG arrow-tip CustomAnnotation for EACH of the 4 shanks (np2Shafts/np2Tips, sized by np2SyncTips), shown via a new setShankVisuals(isNp2) helper (hides the NP1 single shaft in NP2 mode and vice-versa) called from both renderElectrodeMap and renderRecordSelection. Verified: 4 rectangles side-by-side, each with a pointed tip at the bottom, electrodes inside.
  • FILE ORGANISATION. The one-off CDP driver scripts that had been left in the repo ROOT (cdp_np2_smoke.py, hw_gui_test.py, np2_crossshank_demo.py, np2_crossshank_record.py) were un-staged (git reset) and consolidated into python/tools/gui_scenarios.py (a Gui helper + scenarios np2_smoke/hw1_record/np2_crossshank/mismatch_check), which reuses python/tools/cdp.py (added cdp.wheel() earlier). The root scripts were deleted. Pre-existing root .py files (command_client.py, verify_npx_recording.py, …) were left untouched.
  • DOCS. developer_docs/NextSession-Prompts.md updated (NP2.0 → DONE; new CURRENT task = validate RIPPLE detection for 1.0 AND 2.0, THEN audio of spiking). user_docs/GUI-Guide.md (+ user_docs/images/) = the illustrated end-to-end GUI walkthrough (connect, probe map, live view, record selection, recording) for NP1.0 + simulation. developer_docs/Neuropixels2.0.md, GuiTesting.md refreshed.

19. HARDWARE-ERROR GUI ALERTS (detect + comms + streaming relay) + RIPPLE-TASK SCOPE (2026-07-07, built + tested in-browser)#

Follow-up to the §17 probe-type-mismatch dialog: give the SAME blocking-dialog treatment to the other "the profile needs hardware that isn't there" cases, so a failed connect is unmistakable instead of a quiet red toolbar note (and a config naming an unimplemented source no longer sits half-connected in silence).

  • New GUI kind "hardware-error" → showHardwareError modal (webinterface/main.js), a line-for-line sibling of showProbeMismatch: amber ⚠ "Hardware not detected" card with the message + Disconnect (reuses the toolbar Disconnect flow → releases + returns to None) and Dismiss (close, read the console). window.guiStatus routes "hardware-error" here (just like "probe-mismatch").
  • Backend emits (all deferred via QMetaObject::invokeMethod(..., Qt::QueuedConnection) so they land AFTER WebSocketServer::onSourceSelection connects sigGuiStatus — same trick as the probe-mismatch / "Found … Probe" readiness emits):
  • PXIe not detected / not brought up — all five NpInterface.cpp initBasestation failure paths: no basestation (np_getDeviceList ≤ 0), slot unresolved (detectedSlot == -1), np_openBS failure, no headstage on any port, no probe on the headstage dock. Each emits a specific sigGuiStatus(…, "hardware-error") before return false.
  • NI-DAQ not detected — NiDaqInterface.cpp NIDAQSource::initDataSource when scanForDataSource() finds no NI-DAQmx device.
  • OneBox / unimplemented source — WebSocketServer::onSourceSelection: the source-registration loop gained an else for when getSourceByName(actualSourceName) is null (the profile names a module NOT in the C++ registry, e.g. a OneBox config — OneBox is not implemented yet). It broadcastStatusEvent( "guiStatus", msg, "hardware-error") directly (no source object to relay from; the browser is already a client), with a OneBox-specific message when the name contains "onebox", else a generic "not available in this build".
  • TESTED (2026-07-07, in-browser). OneBox path end-to-end: added config/OneBoxTest.yaml (profile "OneBox (unimplemented)", source source.npx.onebox — deliberately unregistered). Selecting it drove the real backend → onSourceSelection else → broadcastStatusEvent("guiStatus", …, "hardware-error") → frontend modal showed the OneBox-specific text (+ red toolbar status). All 13 hardware-error messages and all 4 error-kind (red-status) messages were then injected via window.guiStatus(msg, kind): every hardware-error opened the blocking modal with an EXACT text match; every error showed the red toolbar status and did NOT open the modal; Dismiss closes the modal and Disconnect closes it + fires the toolbar Disconnect. Screenshots in build.../bin/RecordingTests/ (err_onebox, err_pxie_basestation, err_nidaq_nodevice, err_no_probe, err_stream_redstatus).
  • The hardware-ABSENT conditions (no basestation / headstage / probe / NI-DAQ) can't be triggered on this box (both probe + NI-DAQ are present), but the OneBox test proves the whole broadcast→guiStatus→modal chain and §17's probe-mismatch proves the deferred source-emit chain; the absent-hardware emits share those exact paths and are compile-verified.
  • Port 8080 gotcha (for next time): it's held by the NI Application Web Server service (NIApplicationWebServer, auto-start, unrelated to DAQmx). SpikesPeak's own HTTP server can't bind it (it just logs "Failed to start HTTP server" and continues — WS:1234 still works). Stopping the service needs an ELEVATED shell (Stop-Service NIApplicationWebServer); this session couldn't elevate, so the GUI test was served from a tiny threaded static server (correct .js MIME + ThreadingHTTPServer) on :8090 while the backend WS ran on :1234 (Config.WEBSOCKET_URL is hard-coded to ws://localhost:1234, independent of page origin, so this works).
  • Broader hardware-comm coverage (user follow-up — "acknowledge EVERY hardware error in the GUI, not just the console"). Beyond the connect-time detection above, the other Neuropixels/NI-DAQ communication error checks that were console-only now also emit to the GUI:
  • Neuropixels (NpInterface.cpp): np_getProbeHardwareID fail (can't read hardware ID), probe-type unsupported, np_init fail, np_arm fail → "hardware-error" (blocking, connect aborts); np_writeProbeConfiguration fail during init → "error" (red status, non-fatal — connect continues); np_setSWTrigger fail at play → "hardware-error" (probe won't actually stream).
  • NI-DAQ (NiDaqInterface.cpp): worker->initialize() fail (device found but its DAQmx tasks/channels couldn't be configured — this is where the per-device DAQmxFailed config errors bubble up) → "hardware-error".
  • STREAMING-LOOP read errors — now relayed too (throttled worker→source relay, user follow-up). The per-tick acquisition loops run in worker threads whose objects (IDataSourceWorker) had no way to reach the GUI. Added IDataSourceWorker::sigDswError(msg, kind) + a reusable throttle helper (reportStreamError()/resetStreamErrors() in IDataSource.hpp): the alert fires only after kStreamErrMinConsecutive=3 consecutive failed reads (ignores a one-tick blip), then re-fires at most once per kStreamErrReemitMs=5 s while the fault persists; a successful read resets the episode. Each source relays it to the GUI with a signal→signal connect(worker, sigDswError, this, sigGuiStatus) (queued across the thread boundary) in its startAcq. Wired in: Npx1Probe (np_readElectrodeData fault → throttled "error"), Npx2MSProbe (np_readPackets non-SUCCESS → throttled "error"; this loop previously logged NOTHING on error), and NIDAQDevice (the terminal Error: label emits ONE "error" directly — no throttle, since that path stops acquisition). Chose "error" (red toolbar status) over the blocking popup so a glitchy stream doesn't spam a modal; trivially switchable to "hardware-error". Frontend rendering for these three messages verified by injection (above).
  • Still console-only (by design): non-fatal sync-matrix routing warnings and teardown/close errors (they don't block acquisition).
  • Files: webinterface/main.js, src/core/IDataSource.hpp (signal + throttle helper), src/modules/neuropixels/NpInterface.cpp, src/modules/nidaq/NiDaqInterface.cpp, src/core/WebSocketServer.cpp, config/OneBoxTest.yaml (NEW). Rebuilt clean. NOT committed.

20. RIPPLE (SWR) DETECTION VALIDATED for BOTH NP1.0 AND NP2.0 (2026-07-07, built + verified on the Windows box)#

The sharp-wave-ripple detector (proc.rippledetector / src/modules/swr/RippleDetector.cpp) now FIRES, with clean 1:1 correlation to injected events, on both probe geometries in simulation — the pre-in-vivo rehearsal for playing a ripple audio file into saline. This also resolves the §14 deferred TODO (one NP1 sim class, sine-vs-ripple by config, instead of the NP2-style h5 source standing in for NP1 ripples).

20.1 The ripple data path — the detector runs DIFFERENTLY per probe (corrected 2026-07-07 per user)#

The detector operates on the LFP band at 2.5 kHz (its ripple-band FIR + envelope filters RippleBandFilterCoefficients2_5kHz / EnvelopeFilter2_5kHz are designed for 2.5 kHz). Its input band, type, and decimation are now config-driven, so it runs differently per probe: - NP1: proc.rippledetector reads the native data.npx.lfp (int16, 2.5 kHz) DIRECTLY — decimation_factor: 1, no proc.lfp in the path. Ripples live in the LFP band; the AP band must NOT be used — a real 1.0 probe high-passes AP (~300 Hz), which attenuates the 150-250 Hz ripple. Reading the native LFP directly (one int16→float cast of channel 0, no extra thread hop, no filter) is also the low-latency closed-loop path. - NP2: only the wideband data.npx.raw (30 kHz) exists, so proc.lfp low-passes it (Bessel −3 dB @ 400 Hz, LFPFilterCoefficients30KHz) into data.lfp (float, 30 kHz) — this low-pass is the anti-alias filter, REQUIRED before decimation (else 300 Hz-10 kHz spikes fold into the ripple band) — and the detector decimates ÷12 → 2.5 kHz (÷12, not ÷10, to land exactly on NP1's native 2.5 kHz LFP rate). - Detector chain (both): ripple-band FIR (150-250 Hz) → rectify → envelope → fire when envelope > mean + z·std (z=5) with a lockout. Detection channel hard-coded to read-out channel 0 (setRippleDetectionChannels, a TODO). - Baseline warm-up ≈ 16 s, now RATE-AWARE: gate = 2500·decimation·baseline_gate_seconds input samples (= 6 s on BOTH NP1@2.5 kHz [15000] and NP2@30 kHz [180000]; the old hard-coded 30000*6 would be a 72 s warm-up at NP1's rate) + 2500·baseline_duration_seconds decimated envelope samples (10 s) → first detection ~16 s. A validation run must Play ≥ ~18 s (detection runs on Play, independent of Record).

Earlier (now-corrected) mistake, recorded for the archive: a first pass claimed "data.lfp must be 30 kHz" and routed NP1 through proc.lfp on data.npx.ap. That fired in sim only because the simulated AP band has no hardware high-pass; on a real probe the AP high-pass attenuates ripples, so it would not translate to the saline/in-vivo test. Corrected to the direct-native-LFP path above.

20.2 What was built#

  1. Config-driven ripple + noise in BOTH sim sources. SimSignalProfile (NP1, SimDataSource) and Sim2SignalProfile (NP2, SimData2Source) had an injectRipples flag + burst code, but initDataSource did Q_UNUSED(cfg) so it could never be turned on. Both now read the source's YAML node (mirroring NiDaqSimSource): inject_ripples + tunable ripple_amplitude/ripple_freq_hz/ripple_period_s/ noise_amplitude (all editable without a rebuild). Shared rippleAt(t) (Gaussian burst, σ 30 ms) + writeSample (per-channel Gaussian noise when noise_amplitude>0, else the original std::fill fast path → plain-sine configs byte-unchanged, regression-tested). A noise floor is essential: a noiseless sine gives the ripple-band baseline std ≈ 0 → meaningless z-threshold; noise_amplitude: 15 → healthy threshold ≈ 46-53. For NP1 the burst goes into the native LFP band (data.npx.lfp, where the detector reads and where a real probe carries ripples — NOT the AP band). Deterministic RNG seed → reproducible.
  2. RippleDetector made probe-configurable + latency-aware. New keys on proc.rippledetector: - input (data-object name) + input_type (int16 | float) → binds EITHER CircularBuffer<int16,PacketInfo> (NP1 native LFP) OR CircularBuffer<float,float> (NP2 derived), reading/casting only channel 0. decimation_factor (1 for NP1, 12 for NP2; default 12). - Baseline/threshold params now editable (were hard-coded; the DSP defaults from Dutta et al. 2018 JNE are preserved): baseline_gate_seconds (6), baseline_duration_seconds (10), z_score_threshold (5), lockout_seconds (0.2). The 6 s gate is now rate-aware (see 20.1). - debug_files (default true for dev) gates the three per-sample waveform dumps (lfp-data.bin / ripple-band-data.bin / envelope-data.bin) — synchronous I/O in the detection hot loop; set false for a low-latency hardware run. ripple-detection-time.bin (the detection record, one write per event, not per sample) is always written.
  3. Two new configs (tagged sim_variant, join the GUI "Simulated Data" picker): - config/SimNpx1Ripples.yaml — NP1: source.npx.simdata (inject_ripples) → display + no proc.lfp + proc.rippledetector (input: data.npx.lfp, int16, ÷1) + recorder. - config/SimNpx2Ripples.yaml — NP2: source.npx.sim2 (inject_ripples) → display + proc.lfp (anti-alias)
    • proc.rippledetector (input: data.lfp, float, ÷12) + recorder. LFPProcessor input was also made configurable (default data.npx.raw) — a harmless generalization the NP1 path no longer needs. The h5 NpxSynthRipples.yaml (no detector keys) uses the defaults → back-compat.

20.3 VERIFICATION (2026-07-07, headless CLI, this Windows box; NPX_HW_ENABLED but hardware-free sim)#

Launch SpikesPeak.exe --no-browser, then spikespeak_cli.py --script "select '<cfg>'; waitready; play; sleep 34; pause; disconnect; quit". Detection times from ripple-detection-time.bin; plotted with the NEW python/tools/plot_ripples.py (lfp/ripple-band/envelope + threshold + injected-vs-detected). - NP1 (SimNpx1Ripples.yaml, direct native LFP): log "input data.npx.lfp ( int16 ) decimation 1 baseline gate 6 s ( 15000 samples)"; threshold 53.4; 9 detections at 16.98…32.99 s; offset to injected burst centres mean 14.5 / max 18.4 ms. This path holds on real hardware (reads the LFP band, no AP-derived hack). - NP2 (SimNpx2Ripples.yaml): "Deriving data.lfp from data.npx.raw"; "input data.lfp ( float ) decimation 12 baseline gate 6 s ( 180000 samples)"; threshold 46.03; 9 detections at 16.99…32.99 s (identical before/after the refactor). - Correlation: injected burst centres t = 1, 3, 5, … s; every detection within ~15 ms of a centre (the fixed offset is the envelope's rising edge, ~15 ms before the peak). 9/9 post-warm-up ripples, zero misses, zero false positives, none during the 16 s warm-up. PNGs in build.../bin/RippleTests/{np1_directlfp,np2*}/. - debug_files: false confirmed: only ripple-detection-time.bin written (the 3 per-sample dumps skipped), detection still fires — the latency switch works. - Back-compat/regression: the h5 NpxSynthRipples.yaml (detector defaults) still fires (19 detections, threshold 203.6); plain-sine SimNpx1.yaml streams + records clean (noise 0 → std::fill path).
