20.6 CHANNEL SELECTION (by electrode) + MULTI-CHANNEL QUORUM + editable params (2026-07-08, built + verified)#

User-requested flexibility on top of §20.1-20.5 (all config now; ripple GUI next). The detector was single-channel (hard-coded read-out channel 0) and mostly hard-coded; it is now multi-channel, electrode- addressed, and fully parameterised.

  • Channel map (abstract, per-probe). IChannelMap (NEW, src/core/IChannelMap.hpp) is the probe-AGNOSTIC contract — channelForElectrode / electrodeForChannel / numChannels / probeName — so SpikesPeak isn't Neuropixels-only in the long run. Each probe FAMILY ships its own concrete map: NpxChannelMap (NEW, src/modules/neuropixels/NpxChannelMap.{hpp,cpp}) owns the Neuropixels wiring (NP1 electrode = channelId + 384*bank; NP2 global electrode = shank*1280 + electrodeId); a future NeuroNexus/other probe adds its own subclass in its own module. ChannelMapService (in IChannelMap.hpp) is the shared blackboard holding the ACTIVE source's shared_ptr<const IChannelMap> (thread-safe; readers hold their own ref). This is the robust option (b) the user picked: the detector resolves user ELECTRODE numbers to buffer channel indices through the REAL applied map (correct for custom cross-bank/shank maps, not just Bank-0 identity). SimulatedDataSource, SimulatedData2Source, and the hardware NeuropixelsSource build + publish an NpxChannelMap on every map change + at connect (cleared on close for the sims). One active source ⇒ one map. (Both new headers + the .cpp are now in the CMake source lists so they appear in Qt Creator.)
  • Channel selection by electrode. detection_electrodes: [E…] on proc.rippledetector → one independent per-channel detector each (own baseline/threshold/filters/state). Resolved to channels at start (map is published by then); if no map is published or an electrode isn't in the applied map, it falls back to identity + warns. Absent ⇒ the old single-channel-0 behaviour (back-compat; the h5 NpxSynthRipples.yaml is unchanged). NP1 [0,10,20] → e0->ch0 e10->ch10 e20->ch20 (block 0 is identity); NP2 [0,50,100] → e0->ch0 e50->ch98 e100->ch196 (VERIFIED against the silicon permutation — proves the map resolution).
  • Quorum stimulation. quorum_count: K + coincidence_window_seconds: W — a true detection → fire() needs ≥ K of the selected channels to cross threshold within a W-second sliding window. Each channel reports once per above-threshold episode. ERROR CHECK (user requirement): you may select MORE channels than K, never fewer — quorum_count > N makes configure() fail with sigIpErrorOccurred (VERIFIED: quorum 5 vs 3 electrodes → "quorum_count (5) exceeds … (3)", processor setup fails, connect aborts).
  • Per-channel lockout + stimulation lockout (lockout_seconds, one value, all channels). After a channel reports, it is locked for lockout_seconds. On a true stimulation, ALL channels are locked until t_stim + lockout_seconds, so a channel that crossed early is held longer (they "re-activate together when stimulation begins"). With no stimulation (quorum not met), only each channel's own lockout applies.
  • Per-channel minimum duration. min_duration_seconds (default 0) — how long a channel must stay continuously above threshold before it reports. VERIFIED: 0.5 s (≫ the ~60 ms bursts) → 0 stimulations; 0.0 → the normal 9.
  • Editable baseline (warm-up). baseline_duration_seconds is the user's mean/std estimation knob (seconds, abstracted from rate); baseline_gate_seconds stays separate (NPX amps settle slowly — a hardware necessity, not a primary knob). z-score + lockout also editable. All times are float seconds (many are ms-scale).
  • VERIFIED (2026-07-08): NP1 Simulated NPX 1.0 Ripples → 3 detectors (baselines 53.4/53.2/52.8), quorum 2/3, 9 stimulations at 16.98…32.99 s. NP2 Simulated NPX 2.0 Ripples → 3 detectors (46.0/46.1/46.1), 9 stimulations. Error-check + min-duration gating as above. Evidence in build.../bin/RippleTests/{np1_quorum,np2_quorum,np2_map,errcheck,np1_minidur}/.
  • FILES (this round): src/core/IChannelMap.hpp (NEW abstract + service), src/modules/neuropixels/ NpxChannelMap.{hpp,cpp} (NEW concrete NPX map); src/modules/swr/RippleDetector.{hpp,cpp} (electrode resolution, quorum, per-channel lockout/min-duration, editable params, error-check); src/modules/neuropixels/{SimDataSource,SimData2Source,NpInterface}.{hpp,cpp} (build + publish an NpxChannelMap); src/core/CMakeLists.txt + src/modules/neuropixels/CMakeLists.txt (register the new files); config/SimNpx1Ripples.yaml + config/SimNpx2Ripples.yaml. Rebuilt clean (exit 0). NOT committed.
  • THREADING (as-built, for the record): each IProcessor gets its OWN worker thread (proc.rippledetector, proc.lfp, proc.npx.display, … each on a dedicated thread). WITHIN the ripple detector's single thread the N per-electrode detectors run SEQUENTIALLY in one loop and the quorum/stimulation decision is made INLINE — it is NOT one-thread-per-electrode + a separate arbiter thread. For a modest channel count this is LOWER closed-loop latency (no cross-thread hand-off for the quorum) and simpler; per-electrode threads would help only at large channel counts and add decision-latency. Revisit if channel counts grow.
  • NEXT: the ripple-detection GUI (select electrodes; show per-channel threshold crossings + the quorum decision; move the detection notification to a separate thread off the hot path).

20.7 RIPPLE-DETECTION GUI + Start/Pause/Edit + NO-CHANNELS sim config (2026-07-08, built + verified)#

The right-side Ripple Detection dock (webinterface/RippleModule.js, self-injecting tab + dock) is built and wired end-to-end (WS contract in developer_docs/RippleGui.md). This round resolved four user feedback items and added a "just show the module" sim config. - Even columns (no overlap). Dock width AND the main-view right-margin both read one CSS var --rd-dock-w, so the main view ends exactly at the dock edge; the grip clamps the var. (Was: dock spanned full width, live view's right column shrank.) - Window-size switch no longer blanks the display. DataStore.updateWindowSize resizes each FIFO IN PLACE (fifoCapacity = newCapacity) instead of replacing the series object (the SciChart renderable stayed bound to the old, no-longer-fed object → blank on 1s→10s→2s). Safe recreate fallback keeps the last N samples. - Enable+Begin → one Start/Pause button + an Edit button. Start: first-ever start trains μ/σ then detects; a start AFTER a pause just RESUMES (baselines kept, no retrain). Pause: stops detection+stim, envelopes keep streaming. Edit (enabled only while paused): unlocks channels/params/z; while running everything is LOCKED (no Ctrl+drag, no param edits) — Start re-locks. Backend rippleState.enabled is the source of truth for the button + lock (so config auto_start reflects correctly). Backend slApplyCommand gained start/pause (+ legacy enable). - New-channel handling = "train-only-new" (Option A, IMPLEMENTED as default; A/B/C still user's call). Adding a channel to an already-trained set (Pause → Edit → add → Start) keeps the existing channels' baselines and trains ONLY the new one, which then joins the quorum. Backend mergeDetectionChannels copies persisting electrodes' Filter/DetectionParams/ChannelDetectState by value, fresh detectors for new electrodes; dropped electrodes discarded; quorum re-clamped. DECISION OUTSTANDING: A (train-only-new, current) vs B (retrain all on any edit) vs C (per-channel choice at edit time). Flipping A→B is ~1 line (reset baselines+origin in setChannels). - NO-CHANNELS sim config (NEW). config/SimNpx1RippleModule.yaml — same NP1 ripple sim as SimNpx1Ripples but detection_electrodes: [] + auto_start: false, so the dock shows the module with ZERO channels and the user drives everything live (Ctrl+drag electrodes in, then Start). Required a backend semantics change: on proc.rippledetector, detection_electrodes key present but empty ⇒ zero channels (module idle, waiting for the GUI), distinct from key absent ⇒ back-compat channel-0. The quorum error-check is deferred when nCh==0 (re-clamped as the GUI adds channels). - PICKER CURATION (2026-07-08, user). The GUI-driven config is now the ONLY ripple option in the "Simulated Data" picker. Removed the sim_variant key from the two auto/injected "(software)" ripple configs (SimNpx1Ripples.yaml, SimNpx2Ripples.yaml) so they drop out of the picker; the files are KEPT (still selectable BY NAME via spikespeak_cli.py for the headless ripple/recording validation — §20.6, RecordingTest). The main source dropdown is a STATIC list in index.html, so removing sim_variant does NOT leak them into the hardware list. GUI config relabelled Neuropixels 1.0 — Ripple Detection (GUI). (Still in the picker as separate, non-"software" options: the h5-replay Neuropixels — synthetic ripples, sine-wave, and NI-DAQ sims. No NP2 GUI ripple config exists yet.) - VERIFIED (2026-07-08, headless WS smoke test, this Windows box): Play → auto-start (3 channels trained, thr ~52-54); pause → enabled=false (0 detections while paused); setChannels[0,10,20,30] while paused → merged to 4 channels; start (resume) → ONLY e30 trained fresh (thr 53.8), e0/e10/e20 kept baselines; 7 detections total, no crash across the runtime channel-set mutations. Frontend node --check clean; deployed. - FILES (this round): webinterface/RippleModule.js (Start/Pause/Edit + lock/merge UI, drag guard), webinterface/DataStore.js (in-place FIFO resize), src/modules/swr/RippleDetector.{hpp,cpp} (start/pause/enable, mergeDetectionChannels, present-but-empty electrodes ⇒ zero channels, deferred quorum check), config/SimNpx1RippleModule.yaml (NEW), developer_docs/RippleGui.md (contract updated). 20.8 DECISION TO PLAN LATER — COMPOSABLE CONFIGURATION (hardware + selectable modules) (2026-07-08, user)#

User flagged (to talk through + plan later, NOT to act on now): the current config model is too rigid. Today each scenario is a hand-written whole-pipeline YAML (source + processors + recorder + every module's params in one file), so every combination is a brand-new file — e.g. SimNpx1Ripples / SimNpx2Ripples / SimNpx1RippleModule differ only by a few module knobs. Desired end state: the user specifies the HARDWARE (real NPX 1.0 / 2.0 / NI-DAQ / a sim source) and then INDEPENDENTLY selects whichever MODULES to attach (ripple detector, spike audio, heatmap, recorder, …), each module carrying its own settings — "flexible where I just specify the hardware and am able to select whatever modules I want to add." Open questions to resolve when we plan this: a. Composition mechanism — base "hardware profile" + selectable "module" fragments merged at load, vs a UI that assembles the pipeline, vs keep monolithic YAML but generate it (yaml anchors/includes? a merge step?). b. Where module defaults live — in the module (code) with per-scenario overrides, vs entirely in YAML. c. Per-hardware input wiring — a module declares its input requirement so the composer wires it correctly without the user knowing (e.g. ripple wants data.npx.lfp int16 on NP1 but data.lfp float on NP2). d. Runtime attach/detach — add a module to an already-running source, vs load-time only (ties into todo "how processors configure themselves" + system-state tracking). e. GUI story — a module picker (checkboxes per available module) driven by this config model. (This is the same theme as todo.txt Major #4 "flexible configuration" + #8 "how processors configure themselves", captured here so it's part of the ripple/GUI planning thread.)

20.9 2-D PER-CHANNEL HDF5 PLAYBACK + NP2 GUI RIPPLE CONFIG (2026-07-08, built + verified)#

Two user asks: (1) get the HDF5 file playback working (per NextSession-Prompts §"playback data format"); (2) make the NP2 version of the GUI ripple config available. - 2-D playback (SynthRippleSource). initData now reads either a 1-D [nSamples] dataset (broadcast to all channels, back-compat) OR a 2-D [nSamples × nChannels] int16 dataset (each channel its own signal). One unified tiling copy fills the 384-wide stored buffer: read-out channel c ← file channel c % nChannels (1-D⇒broadcast, full 384⇒direct, partial⇒repeat, wider⇒truncate). Reads sampling_rate (required) + optional num_channels attrs. The streaming worker was already 384-wide, so no worker change. - Generator (scripts/gen_synthetic_ripples.py) reimplemented on scipy (ghostipy is NOT installed here). Emits 2-D [N × num_channels] int16 = per-channel Butterworth-bandpassed white-noise background (sosfiltfilt, IIR — a multi-thousand-tap FIR was intractable on [N × 384]) + a depth-weighted shared ripple train (Gaussian over channel index, floor 0.7 so EVERY channel incl. ch0 stays detectable). Ripple centre 200 Hz (inside the detector's 150-250 band, below proc.lfp's 400 Hz). Saves sampling_rate + num_channels attrs + a ripple_centers dataset. New args: --num_channels, --noise_std, --depth_floor, gzip by default. - NP2 GUI ripple config (config/SimNpx2RippleModule.yaml, NEW). The NP2 twin of SimNpx1RippleModule.yaml: source.npx.sim2 → proc.lfp → proc.rippledetector (data.lfp float, decimation 12), detection_electrodes: [] + auto_start:false. sim_variant: "Neuropixels 2.0 Multishank — Ripple Detection (GUI)". So the picker now offers BOTH NP1 and NP2 GUI ripple options. - VERIFIED (2026-07-08, headless, this Windows box). (A) Selected "NPX 2.0 Synthetic Ripples" (h5-replay) with the regenerated 2-D file ((180000, 384) int16, 6 ripples, gzip 69 MB): detector fired 7× on playback. 20.10 RIPPLE DETECTOR CONFIG STRICTLY GUI-DRIVEN (2026-07-08, user)#

User reported a bug where the 'Simulated (h5 replay data)' option automatically began training because it defaulted to channel 0 when no channels were selected. To fix this, we removed mResolveByElectrode and the YAML config fallback entirely. Detection electrodes are now strictly GUI-driven (mDetectionElectrodes starts empty). The detection_electrodes config key is ignored, and the detector will idle with 0 channels until the user selects channels via the GUI. Developer docs (RippleDetection.md) were updated to reflect this change.

20.11 RIPPLE DETECTOR PARAMETERS STRICTLY GUI-DRIVEN (2026-07-08, user)#

User requested the removal of all ripple detector parameter parsing from the YAML config files because the GUI is fully functional and these should only be GUI-driven. We removed config file support for quorum_count, coincidence_window_seconds, min_duration_seconds, aseline_duration_seconds, aseline_gate_seconds, z_score_threshold, and lockout_seconds from both the frontend YAML files and the C++ backend (RippleDetector.cpp). Developer docs (RippleDetection.md) were updated to reflect that these parameters are strictly GUI-driven.

20.12 CUSTOM H5 FILE PLAYBACK AND NP1.0 MIRRORING (2026-07-08, user)#

User requested to support playing custom H5 files and to mimic either Npx1 or Npx2 multishank probe structures. SynthRippleSource was updated to read its target output buffer name dynamically and use a GUI-driven QFileDialog file-browser pop-up triggered over WebSocket (selectSource). Two configuration files are now available: Npx1SynthRipples.yaml which outputs to data.npx.ap and runs proc.lfp to mimic NP1.0 multishank, and NpxSynthRipples.yaml which outputs to data.npx.raw to mimic NP2.0. The GUI picker now shows Simulated Neuropixels 1.0 (play file) and Simulated Neuropixels 2.0 (play file).

OUTSTANDING FINDINGS (edge cases / races / smells — NOT yet fixed, flagged for the user): - SynthRippleSource::sPlaybackFile is a mutable global static (set on the main thread in the WS dialog callback, read in initData). One active source ⇒ no data race in practice, but: (a) it is never cleared on teardown, so re-selecting a play-file config and Cancelling replays the OLD file; (b) architecture smell (global lifecycle state). - publishChannelMap() ALWAYS publishes an NP2 map (shank-0 electrodes only), even for the NP1 play-file config → wrong probe geometry there (latent — NP1 variant is disabled). It is not cleared on closeDataSource, so a later source that doesn't publish inherits the stale NP2 map. - "(play file)" configs cannot be driven headlessly — selecting one opens a native modal QFileDialog that blocks until a human picks/cancels; a CLI select hangs. Automated tests must use the non-play-file configs. - Param removal broke the old headless validation runbook — Simulated NPX 1.0/2.0 Ripples now start with 0 channels, so select; play; sleep detects nothing. Validation now requires WS ripple setChannels+start (RippleDetection.md runbook updated; spikespeak_cli.py has no ripple verb — used a raw-WS rd_verify.py). - mResolveByElectrode is now dead (configure no longer sets it; resolveDetectionChannels no longer reads it). Harmless leftover; remove in a cleanup pass. - Envelope "500 ms" is a fixed 13-point cap (13 @ ~25 Hz ≈ 520 ms), not time-based — coarse but functional. - debug_files defaults true incl. on the real-NP2 NpxPxiNiDaq.yaml (bare proc.rippledetector) → per-sample disk I/O in the hot loop; set false for low-latency hardware runs. - Real-NP2 streaming is NOT blocked: NpxPxiNiDaq.yaml has proc.lfp before the bare proc.rippledetector, so the detector's default input:data.lfp binds; with auto_start now false it stays idle until the user Starts it.

20.16 FIX — play-file loads the PROBE MAP + REMAINING TO-DO (2026-07-08, user)#

Bug: selecting a "(play file)" source streamed data but the Probe Map tab was blank. Root cause: the frontend binds the npx-named source module on deviceReady and calls IMRO invokables on it (getImroAsJson, getImroAsImroText, updateImroFromJson, applyMapToHardware, loadImroFromText — see ImroManager.js), but SynthRippleSource implemented none (the CLI's publishChannelMap only fed the BACKEND ChannelMapService, not the viewer). §20.14's channel-map "fix" therefore didn't make the viewer render. Fix: SynthRippleSource now exposes the same Q_INVOKABLE IMRO surface as SimulatedData2Source, returning a DEFAULT probe map by the bound output — NP2 (data.npx.raw, probeType 3, shank-0 bank-A, Np2::electrodeToChannel) or NP1 (data.npx.ap, probeType 1, bank-0 identity), tracked in mIsNp2 (set in initDataSource). Playback tiles the file across all channels regardless of the map, so map EDITS are accepted-but-cosmetic (a status note; no reroute). Rebuilt clean (exit 0, MOC regen for the new invokables). Files: SynthRippleSource.{hpp,cpp} (+Qt JSON/TextStream includes). Manual verify: pick a "(play file)" source → Probe Map tab now renders the (NP2 4-shank) probe.

22. PI 3B+ STIM SERVER LIVE; STIM PARAMS; NI-DAQ ROBUSTNESS; DOCS REORG (2026-07-13/14)#

  • Production stim server is a live Raspberry Pi 3B+ at stimserver@192.168.1.100 running pulsegenerator (replaced the ESP32; same UDP contract, so no SpikesPeak change). SSH via paramiko (ssh.exe cannot pass a password non-interactively). On/Off = GPIO17 = physical pin 11. Setup guide: raspberrypi_pulsegenerator/README.md.
  • FIRED-ACK closed loop: the Pi ACKs after the train, off the realtime path → StimulatorClient readyRead → rippleStimFired → the GUI STIM LED lights only on a confirmed fire, not optimistically.
  • GUI-editable stim pulse params (phase / gap / freq / train length) + a live pulse preview. At 1 Hz with a 500 ms train the Pi clamps to exactly one biphasic pulse per detection — the setting used for all the 100-ripple tests.
  • NI-DAQ connect/disconnect HARDENED so a failed connect releases the single-reservation PXI-6133 (teardownManagers + close-on-fail): fast-fail and recover instead of hanging. A stuck reservation — not a config bug — was the real cause of an NP2 setupSources failure.
  • Docs reorganized (30 → 23 in-book), new NiDaq.md, live stream renamed NIDAQ AI/O.

23. NP2.0 REAL-HARDWARE STREAMING FIXES + THE 100-RIPPLE RECORDING SETUP (2026-07-15/16)#

  • Four bugs froze real-NP2.0 pause/play + pick-channels (blue LED / frozen view), none reproducible in sim: (1) np_setSWTrigger fired before the read loop (FPGA FIFO undrained); (2) Npx2MSProbe was missing the mCurrWriteInd ring-index seed NP1 already had; (3) DisplayProcessor never re-baselined the timebin after the re-trigger hardware-timestamp reset; (4) NP2 raw needs AC-coupling or it drifts off-screen. Built + verified live. Detail: developer_docs/Neuropixels2.0.md.
  • Recording setup for analysable ripple tests: record ONLY the detector's resolved read-out channels (setRecording gained a channels override; NI-DAQ keeps ai0–ai7), plus a new backend ripple sidecar (<name>_ripple.json) written at record start. Stim is on ai4 for BOTH probes.
  • NP1.0 full closed loop re-verified on real recordings; NP2.0 detection verified.

24. NP2.0 CLOSED LOOP VALIDATED + THE SYNC OFF-BY-ONE BUG (2026-07-16/17)#

  • Three NP2.0 tests, 100 online detections each (1 ch / 2 ch quorum / 3-of-4), synthetic ripple in saline: closed-loop latency 1.94 / 2.70 / 3.45 ms, 0 unmatched, sync ~10 ppm. Results: RecordingTests/CLOSED_LOOP_LATENCY.md (NP2.0 tab).
  • The NP2 detector is now reproduced EXACTLY offline from a recorded raw band (2nd-order Bessel data.lfp → every 12th sample → ripple/envelope FIRs → online threshold → quorum state machine). This supersedes the earlier raw÷12 + DC-removal approximation, which was wrong.
  • Sync off-by-one bug FOUND + FIXED. Test 3's "orphan" stim pulses were an offline bug, not hardware: the NI-DAQ captured one fewer sync pulse (78 vs 79), so match="end" mis-paired a periodic 1 Hz train by a whole period. Invisible in every scalar metric (identical intervals, 0.012 ms residual). sync.py now defaults to match="auto" (nearest-edge). Detail + lessons: developer_docs/RippleClosedLoopAnalysis.md.

25. NP1.0 CLOSED-LOOP REGRESSION PASSED + FIXES + REPO/DOC CLEANUP (2026-07-20)#

25.1 The regression — PASSED#

Re-ran the NP2 protocol on a real NP1.0 + NI-DAQ + the live Pi, to prove the NP2-focused commit did not break NP1. Three tests, 100 online detections each, electrodes auto-picked 160/304/208/112:

test detector online det. ai4 pulses closed-loop latency sync
np1_1channel_1required 1 ch, quorum 1 100 99 1.99 ms (1.37–3.48, n=99, 0 unmatched) 9.8 ppm / 0.010 ms
np1_2channel_2required 2 ch, quorum 2 100 100 2.60 ms (2.01–3.47, n=100, 0 unmatched) 9.8 ppm / 0.009 ms
np1_4channel_3required 4 ch, quorum 3-of-4 100 100 3.10 ms (2.43–4.27, n=100, 0 unmatched) 10.5 ppm / 0.011 ms

Within ~0.35 ms of NP2 at every quorum, same monotonic rise with quorum size. Every item on the regression surface was checked claim-by-claim (shared sync.py, the DisplayProcessor re-baseline, the channels override, NpInterface, NetworkClient AC-coupling). Full write-up: RecordingTests/CLOSED_LOOP_LATENCY.md (NP1.0 tab). Driver: ripple_tools/run_np1_tests.py, which imports NP2's pick_channels/run_test so both probes take one code path.

Two count mismatches were chased down numerically and are benign — a recording that starts before the detector arms produces a lead-in of unstimulated ripples, and the synthetic play file itself drops every tenth ripple (visible identically in the crossings AND the pulses). How to tell those apart from a real detector failure is written up in developer_docs/RippleClosedLoopAnalysis.md.

25.2 Two bugs found by the regression — both FIXED#

  • Ripple sidecar could freeze zero thresholds (a race). The sidecar was a one-shot snapshot at record start; a recording that began before the baselines finished training kept threshold: 0, trained: false forever (hit NP1 test 1 and all three NP2 tests — but NOT NP1 tests 2/3, which is what proved it was a race and not a broken writer). Now the recording target is remembered and the sidecar is rewritten when the last channel finishes training, cleared on record stop (slClearRippleSidecar), and carries baselines_trained + a stim block.
  • The GUI ripple dock did not follow a remote-driven detector. rippleState omitted per-channel z and the stim pulse train, so driving the rig over WS left the dock showing freq 100 Hz / z 3.00 while the detector really ran at 1 Hz / z 2.50. rippleStateJson() is now a member function reporting z/trained/mu/sigma/threshold + the four stim fields, and RippleModule.onState() applies them. Rule: anything the dock displays must be in rippleState. Verified in sim by ripple_tools/verify_sidecar_and_guisync.py (sets values over WS only, reads the rendered DOM back via CDP).

25.3 Filters validated + the debug .bin dumps gated off#

  • The ripple-band and envelope filters are correct — re-ran both FIRs offline over the backend's own dumped input and compared against its own output: relative error ~2e-7 (float32 rounding), correlation 1.000000000, both stages, two channels. Tool: ripple_tools/validate_filters.py.
  • The per-sample .bin dumps are now behind compile-time kill switches, both shipping FALSE: DEBUGGING_RIPPLE_DETECTION_FILTERS (src/modules/swr/RippleDetector.cpp) and DEBUGGING_LFP_FILTERS (src/modules/lfp/LFPProcessor.cpp). Being constexpr, the file creation, every write and the close all compile out — no cost on the detection hot path, and nothing lands in the working directory. Verified: an identical debug_files: true run that used to emit 1 MB dumps now writes nothing. Doc: developer_docs/FilterDebugging.md.

25.4 Repo cleanup + conventions (user-directed)#

  • Deleted (~300 MB): five _cprof_*/ throwaway Chrome CDP profiles, docs_site.zip, transient run logs (_np1_backend.log, _np1tests.log, _np2tests.log, _build.log, _flagcheck.log, stream_endurance.log), the four root filter .bin dumps, the ripple_tools/shots|figs|captures/fix_verify artifact dirs, and the unused design artifacts webinterface/ripple_mockup.html and webinterface/ripple_selftest.png (never referenced by code or built; the real dock screenshots live in user_docs/RippleDetection.md). KEPT: output.log (the backend's only log sink — it is a windowless GUI-subsystem process, so qDebug never reaches a pipe), all RecordingTests/ data, and the commented-out-in-CMake stubs src/Avx.*, src/arch/x86_64/win/AvxSupport.asm, TestFilter.cpp (kept per user).
  • ripple_demo_np1/ renamed to ripple_tools/ — it is neither NP1-only nor a demo; it is the ripple closed-loop toolkit for both probes. All 36 references across the docs updated; the directory is now git tracked (18 scripts + a README).
  • Artifact policy (new standard): analysis figures and the written-up result are durable and live in RecordingTests/<session>/analysis/; anything under ripple_tools/ (screenshots, figs, captures) is transient verification evidence, .gitignored, and deleted once the result is written up.
  • Doc consolidation: developer_docs/StimClosedLoop_Status.md (superseded by §21–§25 above plus the living topic docs) and the completed NextTask_NP1RippleRegression.md were archived to developer_docs/archive/, which now has a README explaining how to catch up and a table of what superseded each retired file. developer_docs/README.md gained a START HERE path for a new agent, a verification-culture section, the artifact policy, and the archiving policy.

25.5 Open items#

  • The wait_baselines helper can return before training truly completes, which is what produces the recording lead-in above. The sidecar now self-corrects, so this is cosmetic; tightening it to wait on the "training complete" state would make recordings start clean.
  • developer_docs/archive/NextTask_SyncPulseCountCheck.md — the NI-DAQ drops a trailing sync pulse vs the NPX. Low priority; the analysis side is already handled by match="auto".

26. ESP32-S3-ETH STIM SERVER — an AM 2300 twin of the Pi (2026-07-20)#

New firmware esp32s3eth_am2300_pulsegenerator/ for the Waveshare ESP32-S3-ETH (W5500 Ethernet), giving a second, interchangeable AM Systems 2300 stim server. It is a behavioural twin of the Pi build, so SpikesPeak needs no change — point the Ripple dock's stim-server field at the board and the whole closed loop (including the confirmed-fire red LED) behaves identically.

Why a new file rather than editing esp32s3_pulsegenerator/. The old sketch targets a different board and is not an AM 2300 driver at all: it emitted a single fixed 60/10 biphasic pulse from ONE pin via RMT — no Sign / Pulse-Baseline lines, no train, no parameters, no ACKs — and drove an Adafruit NeoPixel. It is left untouched for the old hardware.

  • Pin conflict found and avoided. The old sketch's STIM_PIN = 13 is the W5500 SCLK on the ESP32-S3-ETH; using it would fight the Ethernet bus. Stim lines are now GPIO 15 (On/Off) / 16 (Sign) / 17 (Pulse/Baseline) — in Waveshare's own usable-IO list, clear of W5500 (9–14), microSD (4–7), the WS2812 (21), the PSRAM-occupied 33–37, and the boot-strap/USB pins. SPI is remapped to this board (SCLK 13 / MISO 12 / MOSI 11 / CS 14) with a W5500 hardware reset on GPIO 9. NeoPixel code removed.
  • Protocol parity: connect→armed, quit→disarmed (aborts a running train), fire / fire p g f t / fire p f t → fired after the train. Defaults 60 µs / 10 µs / 100 Hz / 500 ms. total_pulses = total_ms×freq/1000 clamped to ≥1, so 1 Hz + 500 ms = exactly one pulse per detection — the setting the closed-loop tests depend on. The fired ACK goes to the sender captured when the fire arrived (a mid-train parsePacket for the abort check would otherwise redirect it).
  • Verified, not assumed: compiles clean for esp32:esp32:esp32s3 (arduino-cli 1.5.1, esp32 core 3.3.10, Ethernet 2.0.2; 26% flash). The fire_pulse_train bodies of the Pi and ESP32 sources were extracted and diffed normalized: the phase/gap/polarity sequence, train maths, min-1 clamp and abort placement are identical, leaving only five intentional differences (bool vs int return, µs vs ns period unit, esp_timer_get_time vs clock_gettime, a per-pulse FreeRTOS critical section standing in for the Pi's mlockall/SCHED_FIFO/isolcpus, and a period hold that sleeps then spins the last 2 ms so an MCU watchdog cannot trip).
  • NOT yet hardware-tested — no physical 2300 run. Scope the output (or capture on NI-DAQ ai4) before any animal use; the bench checklist is in esp32s3eth_am2300_pulsegenerator/README.md.
  • Docs book now carries both stim servers as their own sections ("Stim Server (Pi)" / "Stim Server (ESP32)").

27. CLOSED-LOOP LATENCY PROFILING + ASAP DELIVERY FLAG (2026-07-20)#

Full write-up: developer_docs/LatencyProfiling.md. Raw data: RecordingTests/latency_*_1000.{json,png}.

27.1 Per-stage instrumentation#

The backend now times the loop stage by stage and emits a rippleLatency WS event after every CONFIRMED stimulus, plus a latency_us summary in each recording's _ripple.json: sample_proc_us, detector_backlog_us, detect_to_send_us, send_to_ack_us, device_us, detect_to_ack_us. Collected/summarised by ripple_tools/latency_breakdown.py (distributions + histograms). Nothing was added to the pulse path: one steady_clock read per sample, std::atomic counters, and the ACK arithmetic runs in onReadyRead between detection iterations.

The definition is unchanged and still authoritative: closed-loop latency = threshold crossing -> ai4 pulse on the NI-DAQ, measured OFFLINE by analyze_recording.py (1.99/2.60/3.10 ms NP1, 1.94/2.70/3.45 ms NP2). Re-verified 2.60 ms on NP1 test 2 after all of today's edits.

27.2 Where the time goes (n=1000, live Pi, simulation)#

Our compute is free: filters+FSM 2 us, dispatch 4 us. The dominant local term was the detector backlog: median 400 us, max 2000 us, quantised to exact multiples of 400 us = one NP1 LFP sample period. That is the signature of BLOCK DELIVERY: sources commitWrite(n) a whole block and waitForAvailable() drains it, so the oldest sample in a block of N is already (N-1)x400 us stale before any consumer can see it.

Correction to an earlier claim in this project: "have the detector process the newest sample first" was WRONG and would have made things worse -- the detector already processes oldest-first within microseconds of arrival, and cannot skip samples (the FIRs are stateful). The staleness is created UPSTREAM by delivery granularity and cannot be recovered downstream.

27.3 The ASAP flag -- opt-in, per-source#

low_latency: true on a SOURCE node makes it deliver in the smallest blocks the clock allows (SimulatedDataSource::initDataSource -> SimNpx1Worker::setLowLatency, production tick 1 ms -> 0). Default unchanged, so no existing config moves; only configs that actually close a loop pay the CPU.

A/B, n=1000 each (SimNpx1LatencyBaseline vs SimNpx1LatencyAsap, identical but for the flag):

stage (median us) baseline ASAP change
detector backlog 400 (max 2000) 0 (max 400) eliminated
sample -> quorum 2 5 +3
detect -> datagram out 4 12 +8
datagram -> ACK 1312 830 -482
TOTAL detect -> ACK 1316 840 -476 (-36%)
spread (sd) 431 129 3.3x tighter

Caveat, stated so the number is not oversold: the datagram -> ACK gain is not all real stimulus speed-up. The ACK is timestamped in onReadyRead, which runs during processEvents() BETWEEN batches, so with 1 ms batching the backend could sit on an arrived ACK before noticing it. The ai4 edge is unaffected by when we notice the ACK. The defensible claim for the closed-loop definition is the backlog: ~400 us median (up to 2 ms) removed -- ~15% of the measured 2.60 ms.

27.4 Open / not done#

  • The gain is UNCONFIRMED on hardware. It must be re-measured with a NI-DAQ capture; on a real probe the block size is set by the Neuropixels API's packetisation, which bounds how much is recoverable.
  • The real-hardware equivalent knob (NpInterface read cadence) is not implemented -- only the sim source honours low_latency today.
  • device_us is unpopulated: the stock Pi replies with a bare fired. The backend already parses fired <us>, and the ESP32 firmware already computes it, so one line on either device would split "network" from "stimulator execution".

28. SUB-MILLISECOND CLOSED LOOP ON REAL NP1.0 + A CORRECTED LATENCY PIPELINE (2026-07-21)#

PARTLY SUPERSEDED by §29 (same day). The single-channel result below still stands. The quorum rows here came from 100-detection runs that were later deleted and re-measured at 1000 detections, and the 800 us batching figure was a median of a quantised counter — §29 replaces both. Read §29 for the current numbers.

Results, figures and method: RecordingTests/CLOSED_LOOP_LATENCY.md (its own "Closed-Loop Latency" section in the docs book). Per-test write-ups: RecordingTests/NP{1,2}_RIPPLE_TESTS.md.

28.1 ASAP delivery on real hardware -- sub-millisecond#

low_latency: true now works on the REAL probe, not just the simulator: Npx1Probe::setLowLatency() polls np_readElectrodeData on every event-loop pass instead of every 1 ms, so each commitWrite carries the fewest LFP samples possible. Parsed by NeuropixelsSource::initDataSource; config Npx1PxiNiDaqAsap is identical to Npx1PxiNiDaq apart from that key. Default unchanged.

1000 detections per condition, single channel, synthetic ripple in saline:

baseline (1 ms poll) ASAP change
median 1.483 ms 0.977 ms -506 us (-34 %)
IQR 1.234-1.716 ms 0.922-1.036 ms 3.4x tighter
p95 1.938 ms 1.133 ms -805 us
data batching (live) 800 us 0 us eliminated
n / unmatched 1000 / 0 997 / 0 --

The detector's own compute is ~7 us. The loop is dominated by data delivery and the network/stimulator path, so the batching term was the only local lever -- and it is now gone.

28.2 A clock-scale bug in the offline pipeline -- ALL PRIOR LATENCY NUMBERS WERE INFLATED#

online_det_recording_times mapped the detector's lfpTs (computed as ticks / NOMINAL 100 kHz) onto the recording clock using a t0 derived from the MEASURED per-sample tick rate. Mixing the two conversions left a ~10 ppm SCALE error, so apparent latency RAMPED with recording length: +6.5 ms over a 618 s run (slope 10.66 us/s, r = 0.9875). Invisible on the ~70 s recordings used previously.

Fixed by converting detections back to hardware ticks and mapping with the measured rate -- one conversion, no scale error. After the fix the drift is gone (r = 0.08) and two independent recordings agree (1.483 / 1.425 ms).

Corrected results (all recording tests re-analysed):

test was now
NP1 1 ch / 2 ch / 4 ch 1.99 / 2.60 / 3.10 ms 1.48 / 1.48 / 1.50 ms
NP2 1 ch / 2 ch / 4 ch 1.94 / 2.70 / 3.45 ms 1.15 / 1.18 / 1.17 ms

The previously reported "latency rises with quorum size" was an ARTEFACT of this bug -- the three tests had different durations. Latency is flat across quorum on both probes, which is what the mechanism predicts: quorum decides which crossing fires, not the delivery delay.

28.3 Documentation + housekeeping#

  • New docs-book section "Closed-Loop Latency" (RecordingTests/latency/), results-first, with light/dark figure pairs that follow the site theme (.figpair + .fig-light/.fig-dark in scripts/docs_style.css; both palettes validator-checked against the site's own panel colours).
  • NP{1,2}_RIPPLE_TESTS.md rewritten as results documents (configuration, latency, integrity), with the debugging history dropped.
  • Deleted np1_1channel_1required (duplicate of np1_lat1k_baseline) and np2_1channel_1required (superseded by the upcoming NP2 1000-detection baseline/ASAP pair) -- 635 MB.
  • .gitignore: raw RecordingTests .bin data is NOT version-controlled; the write-ups, each test's analysis/ figures + _summary.json, sidecars and WS captures are.

28.4 Next#

NP2.0 ASAP measurement (1000 detections, baseline + ASAP) to see whether it also goes sub-millisecond.

29. THE FULL NP1.0 QUORUM × DELIVERY MATRIX AT 1000 DETECTIONS + A SECOND SYNC BUG (2026-07-21)#

The 100-detection quorum runs from §28 were deleted and re-measured at 1000 detections in both delivery modes, so the matrix never needs rerunning. Four fresh recordings, ~10 min each, fresh backend per condition, graceful teardown throughout.

29.1 The matrix — every cell ~1000 delivered stimuli, 0 unmatched#

detector default ASAP saving
1 ch 1.483 ms (n=1000) 0.977 ms (n=997) -506 us (-34.1 %)
2 ch, quorum 2 1.489 ms (n=999) 0.942 ms (n=996) -547 us (-36.7 %)
4 ch, quorum 3-of-4 1.484 ms (n=998) 0.963 ms (n=995) -521 us (-35.1 %)

Latency is flat across quorum to within 6 us (default) and 35 us (ASAP) — much tighter than the 100-detection runs could show. ASAP is sub-millisecond everywhere and tightens the IQR 4-5x (482→114, 500→99, 503→116 us). The batching counter reads exactly 0 in every one of ~1000 events in all three ASAP runs.

Electrodes are auto-picked per run, so paired conditions are NOT electrode-matched (2 ch: e80,160 vs e32,64; 4 ch: e32,64,80,160 vs e64,128,144,160). Documented rather than hidden; the effect reproduces across all of them.

29.2 A SECOND sync off-by-one — the wrap bug (np1_4ch_q3_asap)#

One of the four recordings came back with 257 of 995 pulses unpaired and a physically impossible 0.433 ms minimum. It was NOT the hardware and NOT a repeat of the 2026-07-17 match="end" bug.

When the NI-DAQ drops a sync pulse at the start of the train, both the start- and end-anchored offsets in _pair_edges_auto are wrong by a whole period (measured: off_start=+0.950 s, off_end=+1.950 s), so picking the smaller-magnitude one still shifted every NI-DAQ timestamp by exactly 1.000 s. Sync diagnostics look perfect either way — 617 pulses, 9.78 ppm, 0.012 ms residual.

Fix: reduce the coarse offset modulo the sync period into (-ipi/2, +ipi/2], with a warning when it fires (python/spikespeak_postprocess/sync.py). Two clocks started by one host command are offset by milliseconds; any whole-period component is a pairing artefact. After the fix: unmatched 257 → 0, median 0.963 ms, and the three healthy recordings are bit-identical to before.

How it was caught, and the generalisable lesson. The measured detection→pulse offset was 1001.0 ms with a p5–p95 spread of only 0.3 ms — 1.000 s plus the true ~1 ms latency. On a strictly periodic 500 ms stimulus train a whole-period shift still pairs every pulse to a detection at ~1 ms and looks perfect. It only became visible at the aperiodic landmarks — the ~1.6 s gaps between ripple bursts, where the last two pulses of each burst had no detection. Never validate a periodic signal by nearest-neighbour pairing; anchor on the aperiodic structure. Both plotting scripts and the analyzer now cross-check burst-anchored against nearest-before pairing, and they agree on all nine recordings.

29.3 Stage decomposition was using medians — now means#

The batching counter is quantised to one input sample (400 us at 2.5 kHz), so its median snaps to 0 / 400 / 800 us: two runs whose totals matched to 5 us reported medians of 400 us and 0 us. Medians are also not additive, so subtracting them from a median total pushed the discretisation error into the network + stimulator residual (1082 vs 1476 us for those same two runs). Both plot_latency_figure.py and plot_latency_compare.py now use means, which are additive, and the residual is labelled as obtained by subtraction.

Open item (documented, not hidden): the batching mean falls monotonically with channel count (631 / 381 / 214 us for 1 / 2 / 4 ch) while the NI-DAQ-anchored total stays flat at 1.483-1.489 ms, so the residual rises to compensate. The totals are trustworthy; this channel-count dependence of the attribution is unexplained. The stage split is indicative, not exact.

29.4 Housekeeping#

  • Deleted the four superseded 100-detection quorum recordings (1.55 GB) and their figures.
  • All nine recordings re-analysed with the fixed sync; NP2.0 numbers unchanged (1.179 / 1.173 ms), confirming the wrap bug hit only the one recording.
  • New figures, light + dark: per-run 5-panel for all six NP1 conditions, plus asap_comparison, asap_comparison_2ch, asap_comparison_4ch in RecordingTests/latency/.
  • CLOSED_LOOP_LATENCY.md, NP{1,2}_RIPPLE_TESTS.md and LatencyProfiling.md updated; the "Closed-Loop Latency" material merged into the docs-book Hardware Results section.

29.5 Next#

NP2.0: the same 1000-detection baseline + ASAP pair, to see whether it also goes sub-millisecond. Hardware swap to the 2.0 probe is the gate.

30. NP2.0 ASAP + THE FULL NP2 MATRIX AT 1000 DETECTIONS (2026-07-21)#

Same 1000-detection quorum × delivery matrix as §29, now on the NP2.0 multishank probe. Six fresh recordings, fresh backend per condition, graceful teardown throughout. Results/figures/method: RecordingTests/CLOSED_LOOP_LATENCY.md (the consolidated tabbed NP1/NP2 hardware-results page).

30.1 ASAP had to be implemented for NP2 first#

The low_latency flag was wired only to Npx1Probe — an NP2 ASAP config would have been a silent no-op, producing fake "ASAP == baseline" numbers. Added Npx2MSProbe::setLowLatency() (the same setInterval(mLowLatency ? 0 : 1) timer change), forwarded it in NeuropixelsSource::startAcq, and created config/Npx2PxiNiDaqAsap.yaml. Rebuilt; verified from the backend log that condition 1 ran at poll interval 1 ms and condition 2 at 0 ms (low_latency = true) before trusting the A/B.

30.2 The matrix — sub-millisecond on NP2, 0 unmatched everywhere#

detector default ASAP saving
1 ch 1.351 ms † 0.600 ms -751 us (-55.6 %)
2 ch, quorum 2 1.186 ms 0.608 ms -578 us (-48.7 %)
4 ch, quorum 3-of-4 1.124 ms 0.546 ms -578 us (-51.4 %)

NP2 is faster than NP1 in BOTH modes (~0.3 ms default, ~0.37 ms ASAP) — the probe→detector path (NP2 software-derives LFP from the fresh 30 kHz raw; NP1 waits on the hardware LFP band), consistent with the long-known baseline gap now shown to carry into ASAP.

30.3 The batching counter under-reports the ASAP benefit on NP2 (mechanistic finding)#

On NP1 the detector-side data batching counter (631→0 us) matched the total saving, because NP1 reads data.npx.lfp directly so the poll latency IS the detector-input backlog. On NP2 the detector runs off data.lfp derived by proc.lfp from the raw poll, so the 1 ms raw-poll latency sits UPSTREAM of the detector where its input counter can't see it. The counter shows only ~230→60 us, but the NI-DAQ total drops ~578-751 us and the net+stim residual (a near-fixed ~0.9 ms floor on NP1) collapses ~0.4 ms — that collapse is the removed upstream raw-poll latency. Lesson: instrument the SOURCE poll, not just the detector input, when the detector eats a derived stream. NI-DAQ total is authoritative.

30.4 Verification + one caveat#

All six cross-checked with the independent burst-anchored pairing; the three ASAP medians agree with nearest-before to <= 7 us. † np2_1ch_baseline alone shows mild sync-clock curvature (linear residual 0.077 ms vs 0.015-0.028 for the rest; a quadratic fit halves it but the ~0.1 ms nb-vs-burst gap persists, so it's a within-burst effect not a pure sync-fit error). Its burst-anchored median is 1.23 ms → read the 1-ch baseline as ~1.2-1.35 ms. No ASAP cell is affected. Graceful teardown verified: FPGA confirmed idle (backend exited) before any analysis touched the host.

30.5 Next#

Both probes are now fully characterised end to end. Candidate next steps: NP2 offline exact-detector reconstruction on the new recordings; the ESP32-S3-ETH stim path once a working board is in hand. (The fuller open-items backlog is §31.)

31. OPEN ITEMS / BACKLOG — folded from the retired todo.txt (2026-07-21)#

Almost every original item is done (flexible YAML config, logging, console input, NP1/NP2 acquisition + streaming + recording, LFP, ripple detection + closed-loop stim, versioning, docs, benchmarks, AVX detection). The genuinely-open items it still named, now tracked here:

  • A generic command/event subscription bus — todo.txt's old items 5–6 ("formats of commands/events" + "how to subscribe to commands/events"). The single still-live design item: it does NOT exist yet, and today is approximated by config-wired data streams + Qt signals/slots + per-module Q_INVOKABLE + the vestigial CommandDispatcher. Design brief: NextSessionPrompts/01_command_event_subscription.md; current-state analysis: architecture review §11.6; the module-command standard is developer_docs/ModuleCommunication.md. Solution options + recommended plan (2026-07-24): developer_docs/NextTask_CommandEventBus.md — the open design questions are collected in §31.1 below.
  • More probes — Neuropixels OneBox is not implemented (source.npx.onebox unregistered; selecting the profile raises a hardware error). NP1.0 and NP2.0 multishank are done; NP2 single-shank parses/serializes only.
  • Spike processing — spike-band filtering and spike detection processors are not built (LFP filtering and ripple detection are).
  • Build / infra — build-as-a-library and pre-compiled headers (todo.txt "Minor" 1–2) remain undone.
  • Fuller FDIR — processor error → frontend is partial (recordingStatus + hardware-error alerts exist); a complete error/invalid-state strategy is still future work (architecture review §11).

The original todo.txt checklist text is preserved in git history; the file itself is now a short pointer here.

31.1 Open design questions for the command/event bus — resolve before building (2026-07-24)#

A full design brief with four solution options, a recommended MessageBus plan, phased steps, and a measured validation now lives in developer_docs/NextTask_CommandEventBus.md. The recommendation is Option B — a new MessageBus sibling that supersedes CommandDispatcher: string cmd.*/evt.* topics, JSON-in-QByteArray payload, Qt::QueuedConnection delivery to each subscriber's own thread, and a global getMessageBus() cleared on teardown. The following are left open for the team to settle before a future agent implements it (a suggested answer is in parentheses, to accept or override):

  1. Topic granularity — flat topic strings only, or wildcard/prefix subscriptions (evt.ripple.*)? (Suggest: start flat; add prefix matching only if a real need appears.)
  2. Command-vs-event marker — an explicit kind enum, or the cmd.* / evt.* naming convention? (Suggest: the naming convention — simpler and self-documenting in YAML.)
  3. CommandDispatcher disposition — retire it fully, or thin-shim it onto the new bus for one release? (Suggest: full removal — its one subscriber is inert and its one dispatch path is dead code.)
  4. Payload contract — JSON-in-QByteArray for every structured topic, or allow raw binary for specific high-rate topics? (Suggest: JSON by default; revisit only for a measured high-rate case.)
  5. Handler contract — one virtual slOnBusMessage(topic, payload) per module, or per-topic named handlers declared in YAML? (Suggest: the single virtual slot — minimal boilerplate.)

Status: not started — for discussion between the maintainers, then hand to a future agent.

32. HTTP UI PORT MOVED 8080 → 8234 (SpikesPeak-specific) (2026-07-21)#

The backend's static-UI HTTP port was hardcoded to 8080, which on the Windows rig is permanently held by the NI Application Web Server service (NIApplicationWebServer, auto-start, part of the NI stack — the §23 "port 8080 gotcha"). SpikesPeak's own HTTP server therefore couldn't bind it (it logs "Failed to start HTTP server" and continues, so WS:1234 still works but the UI won't load). Changed the hardcoded port to 8234 — a SpikesPeak-specific port that echoes the :1234 WebSocket and is clear of NI's :8080 (src/main.cpp, httpPort). The WebSocket port (:1234) is unchanged, so the backend no longer collides with NI at all (the NI service can stay running).

  • Updated every reference to http://localhost:8234: the docs (developer_docs/, user_docs/, images/architecture_overview.md, images/architecture.txt), the spikespeak skill, and the CDP / benchmark helpers (python/tools/gui_scenarios.py, benchmarks/). 31 occurrences across 14 files.
  • Requires a rebuild to take effect. The rendered architecture SVGs still show :8080 until regenerated from the (updated) .txt sources.
  • Left as a fixed port (not configurable); a --http-port flag / SPIKESPEAK_HTTP_PORT env override is a possible future enhancement if a machine ever collides with :8234 too.

34. NP2 NI-DAQ DIGITAL-INPUT PARITY + RIPPLE DETECTION DEFAULTS TO ASAP; COMMAND/EVENT BUS BRIEF (2026-07-24)#

Three pieces of work this session.

34.1 Command/event subscription bus — design brief + open questions (design only, nothing built)#

Wrote the design brief for the one still-live architectural TODO (§31, the retired todo.txt items 5–6): a config-declared, runtime-modifiable, unified command + event subscription bus that dispatches off the hot path. It inventories the four current mechanisms (config-wired data plane, hardcoded Qt signals, Q_INVOKABLE, the vestigial CommandDispatcher), gives four solution options with trade-offs, a recommended MessageBus plan (string cmd.*/evt.* topics, JSON-in-QByteArray, Qt::QueuedConnection delivery to each subscriber's thread, getMessageBus() cleared on teardown), phased steps, and a measured validation → developer_docs/NextTask_CommandEventBus.md (indexed under "Open task briefs"). The open design questions are collected in §31.1 above for the maintainers to settle before a future agent builds it.

34.2 NP2.0 + NI-DAQ now streams DIGITAL input (AI + DI parity with NP1.0)#

The NP1.0+NI-DAQ config (Npx1PxiNiDaq.yaml) streamed both NI-DAQ analog (data.nidaq.raw) and digital (data.nidaq.di) inputs; the NP2.0+NI-DAQ configs streamed analog only. Closed the gap — a config-only change, because the real NIDAQSource already produces data.nidaq.di probe-agnostically (the DI producer + per-line 0/1 mirror in NiDaqInterface.cpp; data.nidaq.di + interp.nidaq.di are registered on all platforms). Added the three DI blocks — source outputs data.nidaq.di, a proc.nidaq.display input at scale: 5.0, and a proc.nidaq.recorder recording (interp.nidaq.di, nidaq_di, channels 0–7 @ 40 kHz) — to NpxPxiNiDaq.yaml and Npx2PxiNiDaqAsap.yaml, keeping them "identical except low_latency". Both parse-verified. (The sim NI-DAQ source.nidaq.sim still carries digital only as the analog packet's digitalBitmask — no first-class data.nidaq.di; unchanged, a real-source-only stream.)

34.3 Ripple detection now defaults to ASAP — and NP2 sim now honours the flag#

low_latency (ASAP delivery: source poll 1 ms → 0) is a per-source flag, code default false. Made the ripple detector default to ASAP in two parts: - Config: set low_latency: true on the NPX source of the everyday ripple configs — SimNpx1Ripples, SimNpx2Ripples, SimNpx{1,2}RippleModule, SimNpx{1,2}NiDaqRipples, SimNpx2RealNiDaqRipples. Left the baseline↔ASAP A/B experiment pairs untouched (SimNpx1LatencyBaseline/Asap, and the PxiNiDaq baselines vs their *Asap twins) so the latency methodology is preserved. - Code: the NP2 sim worker SimNpx2Worker::slStartAcquiringData hardcoded setInterval(1) and silently ignored low_latency, so ASAP had no effect in NP2 simulation. Fixed it to mirror the NP1 sim exactly — parse in SimulatedData2Source::initDataSource, forward via setLowLatency in startAcq, setInterval(mLowLatency ? 0 : 1) + the confirming production tick interval 0 ms (low_latency = true) log line (src/modules/neuropixels/SimData2Source.{hpp,cpp}). Real NP2 hardware already delivered ASAP via Npx2MSProbe::setLowLatency; NP1 (sim + hw) already honoured it.

Build-verified (MSVC2019 incremental via _rebuild.bat, exit 0: the changed TU + Utils.cpp + MOC recompiled and SpikesPeak.exe linked). Runtime A/B not re-measured — the change is a line-for-line mirror of the validated NP1 ASAP path and self-reports the tick interval in the log. LatencyProfiling.md updated (it had said the flag was "opt-in / off by default" and that "no existing config changes behaviour").

34.4 Hardware verification — ASAP confirmed LIVE on the real NP2.0 + NI-DAQ (2026-07-24)#

Ran the built backend headless against the real NP2.0 multishank probe + NI-DAQ (Npx2PxiNiDaqAsap) via spikespeak_cli.py --script (connect → waitready → play → 5 s record → stop → pause → disconnect → graceful quit). Confirmed for an imminent in vivo session: - Compiles + runs: python scripts/spikespeak.py build → exit 0 (ninja no-op, exe current); backend up, Found Neuropixels 2.0 Multi-Shank Probe, deviceReady. - ASAP active on real NP2: [Npx2MSProbe] acquisition poll interval 0 ms (low_latency = true) — the config's low_latency: true reaches Npx2MSProbe::setLowLatency, exactly the latency-profiling expectation. - Correct rates: NP2 raw 30 kHz × 384 ch + NI-DAQ 40 kHz × 8 ch — proven by a 5 s recording (raw ≈120 MB ⇒ ~162 k samples ⇒ ~5.4 s @ 30 kHz; NI-DAQ ⇒ ~5.4 s @ 40 kHz). - NI-DAQ AI + DI both live on hardware: Stream nidaq ready: 8 channels + Stream nidaq_di ready: 8 channels; the recording wrote data.nidaq.di/interp.nidaq.di — the §34.2 digital-input parity works on the real rig. - Graceful shutdown: DAQmx tasks closed, FIFO flushed, registries cleared, exit 0; no residual process; no errors/warnings in output.log (the lone "fails" hit reads fails= 0). The 149 MB transient test recording was removed. - ⚠️ Stim note: the detector armed the stimulator on connect (ARM stim -> 192.168.1.100:8888) and no disarm line was logged on quit. No animal was connected and detection never ran (0 channels) so no pulses fired — but confirm the stimulator's state before beginning in vivo.

34.5 Source-architecture reorg — planning brief (design only, nothing built) (2026-07-24)#

Wrote developer_docs/NextTask_SourceArchitecture.md: how to tame the 23-config explosion + the two hand-synced pickers, and cleanly support (2) arbitrary NPX simulation, (3) a NeuroNexus/Intan hardware family, and (4) replay-a-recording-by-hitting-Play. Inventories the 6 source classes / 23 configs / dual selection path, then gives four options — A (config consolidation + one capability-tagged picker), B (unify the two NPX sim classes into one geometry-parameterized NpxSimSource + a general FilePlaybackSource aligned to the recorder's .bin+.bin.json), C (a source-type descriptor registry that kills the name-substring routing), D (a modules/intan/ NeuroNexus source mirroring NIDAQSource) — plus a phased recommendation (A → B → C, D with the hardware). Honours the SimDataLinux.md "one sim class per hardware family" ruling. For the maintainer + collaborator to pick a direction — not implemented.

35. NEUROPIXELS ONEBOX SUPPORT — USB BASESTATION, 12-CH ADC, TIP REFERENCING, INTRO SPLASH (2026-08-18)#

Branch feature/OneBoxSupport. Reference implementation: the local OpenEphys neuropixels-pxi copy (Source-OpenEphys-Neuropixels-PXI/, gitignored — clone from https://github.com/open-ephys-plugins/neuropixels-pxi). Plan + design: developer_docs/archive/NextTask_OneBoxSupport.md.

Headline — the OneBox is a working acquisition source. A real Neuropixels 2.0 multishank probe on a real OneBox connects, detects the headstage, streams, records, pauses/plays, disconnects and — the part that took the longest — reconnects cleanly, with its 12-channel ADC streaming alongside as its own live column in real volts. Everything below marked "verified" was verified on that hardware, not in sim. NP1.0-on-OneBox is untested (it shares the code path; it is the next hardware task, §35.9).

35.1 Why OneBox is a separate source class, not a config flag#

OneBox is the USB (FTDI FT601) sibling of the PXIe basestation: same IMEC NeuropixAPI v4, same probe workers, same CircularBuffer streaming — but a different enumeration and open path, plus hardware PXIe does not have (a 12-ch ADC, a DAC/WavePlayer, its own switch matrix). NpInterface.cpp was already ~2 k lines, so OneBox went in its own pair:

  • src/modules/neuropixels/OneBoxSource.{hpp,cpp} (NEW, 105 + 380 lines) — class OneBoxSource : public NeuropixelsSource. Everything probe-side is inherited; only basestation-specific behaviour is overridden.
  • NpInterface.{hpp,cpp} grew two virtual hooks whose defaults are the existing PXIe behaviour, so the PXIe path is untouched by construction:
  • virtual bool openBasestation() — default: PXIe scan/open. OneBox: np_checkFtdiDriver → np_scanBS → pick the device whose basestationID.platformid == NPPlatform_USB → np_mapBS(serial, 16) → np_openBS.
  • virtual int detectProbePort() — default: the PXIe np_openPort-gated sweep of ports 1–4. OneBox: ports 1–2.
  • Members moved private → protected; initBasestation() STEP 1/STEP 2 now call the two hooks.
  • initDataSource now calls closeDataSource() when initBasestation() fails, so a failed attempt does not leave the device half-open.
  • Registry (src/core/Utils.cpp): source.npx.onebox, data.npx.adc, interp.npx.adc.
  • Configs: config/Npx1OneBox.yaml, config/Npx2OneBox.yaml (NEW). config/OneBoxTest.yaml DELETED — it was a phantom source (referenced an unregistered type, so selecting it only produced the "not supported" dialog).
  • GUI: two new source options, NPX 1.0 (OneBox) / NPX 2.0 Multishank (OneBox).

Driver: OneBox needs FTDI D3XX 1.3.0.10 — not the 1.4.0.x line. Installed by the maintainer this session. Status LED: red = idle / not opened, green = opened. The OneBox lives at virtual slot 16.

35.2 The connect bug: PXIe gates ports with np_openPort, OneBox does not#

The first hardware attempt failed with "No Headstage detected on any port". Root cause: the initial implementation reused the PXIe port sweep verbatim, and OpenEphys's OneBox.cpp calls np_detectHeadStage directly on ports 1–2 — np_openPort is a PXIe-only call and is absent from their OneBox path. That is exactly what detectProbePort() exists to express.

Worth recording because it contradicts the reference: on this OneBox, calling np_openPort best-effort before detectHeadStage is what actually works — detection succeeds on attempt 0 with it. The override therefore does best-effort np_openPort + np_detectHeadStage, wrapped in a retry loop (8 × 500 ms) because the headstage is not always up the instant the basestation opens. Verified live: [OneBox] Auto-detected Headstage at Port: 1 (attempt 0).

35.3 ⚠️ SAFETY — the "bricking" bug, and how OneBox must be closed#

Several times the OneBox came back blinking red with np_scanBS finding nothing — it looked bricked. Two independent causes, both now fixed, both worth knowing:

  1. Hard-killing the backend. The FTDI handle is released only on a clean exit. TaskStop / Stop-Process / taskkill on a backend with an open OneBox leaves the device claimed. This is the same rule already in force for the PXIe probe + NI-DAQ: always shut down via the WS Disconnect/quit path. Force-kill is sim-only.
  2. The close sequence itself was wrong. closeDataSource had been left on the inherited PXIe path, which routes SM_Output_SMA <- SM_Input_PXI0 — i.e. it drives the OneBox's SMA line. The maintainer asked directly whether this matched OpenEphys; it did not. OneBoxSource::closeDataSource now mirrors OneBox.cpp: ADC off → setSyncAsInput() (clear the StatusBit + SMA outputs, then StatusBit <- SMA) → closeProbe → closeBS → np_unmapBS. It deliberately does not call the base implementation.

The maintainer confirmed OpenEphys re-opened the device stably green afterwards — i.e. the device was in a bad configuration, never damaged. Connect → disconnect → reconnect is now verified repeatedly.

35.4 The 12-channel ADC as its own stream#

Decision (maintainer): the OneBox ADC is a stream alongside NI-DAQ, not a replacement — NI-DAQ stays for PXIe rigs and is not wired to the OneBox.

  • OneBoxSource::initDataSource does the base init, then np_ADC_enableProbe, np_ADC_setVoltageRange(ADC_RANGE_5V), and logs np_ADC_getStreamConversionFactor.
  • The ADC runs on its own QThread with its own worker (OneBoxAdcWorker), independent of the probe workers; startAcq/stopAcq own its lifecycle.
  • DisplayProcessor.cpp: num_channels is now per-stream configurable. It was hardcoded 384 in two places (the calcMinMax AVX loop — which also needed a scalar tail — and the advanceBucket serialisation). A 12-channel stream was impossible before this.
  • Scale is real volts, not µV. ChartColumn.isProbeStream() is true only for 384-wide streams, so the 12-ch ADC renders on the NI-DAQ "×V" volts scale; feeding it a µV scale factor overflowed that scale and looked like noise. Now 5.0f/32768.0f; the hardware's own conversion factor reads 0.000152588 V/count, which agrees exactly.
  • The "it's just noise" report was correct behaviour — floating inputs picking up ambient. Grounding the inputs visibly changed the trace, which is the confirmation that the path is real.
  • ChartColumn.streamLabel gained adc: 'OneBox ADC'.

Fixed this session: stopAcq relied on the signal chain alone to stop the ADC thread and logged "worker did not stop within 2s" on every teardown. It now calls mAdcThread->quit() directly — verified 0 warnings.

35.5 Tip referencing — a GUI toggle for NP1.0 and NP2.0 (not OneBox-specific)#

Requested as "relatively important", mirroring OpenEphys. NeuropixelsSource gained Q_INVOKABLE setTipReferencing(bool) / isTipReferencing() + bool mTipReferencing (default false = external reference), written to hardware immediately like Apply Map. On NP2.0 multishank each channel references its own shank's tip. Front end: a #chk-tip-ref checkbox in the probe-map panel (index.html, wired in ImroManager.js).

35.6 PXIe vs OneBox noise matrix — recordings complete#

RecordingTests/2026-08-18_pxie_vs_onebox_noise/ — a 2 × 2 matrix (cells.json): PXIe grounded / ungrounded, OneBox grounded / ungrounded (the OneBox-ungrounded cell is RecordingTests/onebox_np2_baseline/). The OneBox + SMA-grounded cell was recorded this session to complete the set; the quantification was carried by the parallel opus-4.8 agent, whose outputs (figures, noise_metrics.json, noise_table.md, report.html, plus ONEBOX_CONNECT_ISSUE.md and SESSION_CHANGES.md) are in that directory's analysis/.

Repo hygiene: the raw .bin cells total ~240 MB and must NOT be committed — no .bin is tracked under RecordingTests/ today (156 tracked files, 0 .bin). Only analysis/ (3.3 MB) is commit-worthy.

35.7 The intro splash — webinterface/LogoSplash.js (NEW)#

Replaces the old static #logo-watermark. Self-contained; the animation morphs a mountain ridge into an action potential (the NeuralAscension mark into the SpikesPeak mark), with a scan dot tracking the trace and a breathing glow.

Behaviour — iterated with the maintainer, and the final rule is not the obvious one: visibility keys off connected, not streaming. So it shows at startup, pulses "connecting to source…" once a source is picked, fades the instant the device is ready (not on Play), stays gone across pause, and returns on Disconnect — where it covers the empty chart grid instead of leaving bare SciChart panes. Hooks: reflectSource → setSource, handleDeviceReady → setConnected, setPlayingUI → setStreaming; the local Disconnect handler calls setSource(null) (which clears connected) because the C++ side sends no deviceReady on disconnect. The overlay paints #1c1c1c, so .toolbar was raised to z-index:10 to stay reachable underneath it.

Composition, measured rather than eyeballed — the spike apex sits at viewBox x=228 (47.5 %), and every element is pinned to it: the s|P seam is a 2-column grid at 47.5 %, and "by" + the NeuralAscension lockup use padding-right:5% so their content box centre lands on 47.5 % at any viewport. The wordmark is tracked so its ink is symmetric about the apex (Spikes -.025em / Peak +.09em → 169.1 px vs 163.7 px, 5.5 px residual).

Colour — sampled from the rendered trace, not picked. The trace was rasterised to a canvas (glow filter and mountain fill stripped) and sampled column by column. Spikes = the flat baseline's colour #82ba64; Peak = the apex colour #5fd0f0. The previous #92bd4b/#3caed7 were the gradient's raw stops, which the trace only actually reaches at the undershoot trough and mid-flank — so both words were slightly off-tone.

Two colour experiments were tried and rejected, recorded in-file so they are not re-litigated: - Painting the trace's per-x colour onto the letters. Geometrically exact (0.0 px residual) but the spike is narrow, so the whole green→cyan→green swing lands inside ~40 px of a ~350 px wordmark and breaks mid-glyph — it reads as a rendering fault. - Stretching that gradient to the word's scale. Smooth, but it ends "Peak" in green, fighting the name.

Flat two-colour type won, because the colour story is already told structurally (the seam sits on the apex, so each half wears the colour of the trace above it), and flat survives favicon sizes and one-colour reproduction, which gradient-filled letterforms do not. The artwork did take the palette discipline: the lsSpike gradient collapsed from three hues to exactly the two brand colours (0%/35% #5fd0f0, 78%/100% #82ba64), measuring 94.4 % of the trace as pure brand colour, blending only across the limbs. A sweep of that hold offset (22/35/45/55/65/75 %) showed 35 % is the optimum, not a compromise — it is the maximum of the purity column, and 75 % collapses to 22 % purity because the flat baseline sits at 91 % of the bounding box and falls inside the fade.

Timing: morph ~4× slower than the first draft (morphE += 0.003, ~5.5 s) so the mountain actually reads before it sharpens; the scan dot kept brisk (1400 ms connecting / 2800 ms idle) since it represents live signal.

Logo asset: webinterface/na-logo.svg — the maintainer's own export with text converted to outlines, so the Technovier face is not shipped (it is personal-use licensed). webinterface/logoAnimation/ holds the source material the animation was derived from.
